<summary>WeilConjectures/CategoryTheory/RingObjects/Condensed/EllAdic.lean</summary>

```lean
/-
Copyright (c) 2026 Nailin Guan. All rights reserved.
Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.
Authors: Nailin Guan
-/
module

public import WeilConjectures.CategoryTheory.RingObjects.Condensed.ContinuousMap
public import WeilConjectures.CategoryTheory.RingObjects.EllAdic

/-!

# The condensed coefficient rings `ℤ_ℓ`, `ℚ_ℓ`, `𝒪_E`, `E` and `ℚ̄_ℓ`

Fix a prime `ℓ`. The four *topological* `ℓ`-adic coefficient rings are defined as condensed
rings by their sections on profinite sets, following Section 3 of
`WeilConjectures/RefactorInstruction.tex`:
```
ℤ̲_ℓ(S) := C(S, ℤ_ℓ),   ℚ̲_ℓ(S) := C(S, ℚ_ℓ),   𝒪̲_E(S) := C(S, 𝒪_E),   E̲(S) := C(S, E),
```
with restriction by precomposition and the pointwise ring operations: each is
`Profinite.continuousMapRingSheaf` of the corresponding topological ring. Here `E` is a subfield
of `ℚ̄_ℓ = PadicAlgCl ℓ`, given by an embedding `E → ℚ̄_ℓ`, with the topology restricted from
`ℚ̄_ℓ` (`PadicAlgCl.normedFieldOfAlgebra`), and `𝒪_E = PadicAlgCl.integers ℓ E` is its ring of
integers.

The familiar descriptions `ℤ̲_ℓ ≅ lim_n ℤ/ℓⁿ`, `ℚ̲_ℓ ≅ ℤ̲_ℓ[1/ℓ]`, `𝒪̲_E ≅ lim_n 𝒪_E/ϖⁿ` and
`E̲ ≅ 𝒪̲_E[1/ϖ]` are *not* definitions; they are comparison isomorphisms, to be proved later.

The condensed `ℚ̄_ℓ` is not of the form `C(-, T)`. Following Definition 3.6 of the notes, a
section over a profinite set `S` is a continuous map `S → E` into a finite subextension
`E ⊆ ℚ̄_ℓ` of `ℚ_ℓ`, regarded in `ℚ̄_ℓ`: the condensed ring `ℚ̲̄_ℓ` is the subsheaf of
`C(-, ℚ̄_ℓ)` of the maps with values in a finite subextension
(`Profinite.padicAlgClCondensedRing`). The sheaf condition is checked directly: a covering is
refined by a finite jointly surjective family, and finitely many finite subextensions have a
finite compositum. It is *not* `C(-, ℚ̄_ℓ)` for the valuation topology, which has more sections:
a sequence of elements of unbounded degree converging to `0` gives a continuous map on a
convergent sequence that takes values in no finite subextension.

The inclusions `ℤ_ℓ → ℚ_ℓ → E`, `ℤ_ℓ → 𝒪_E → E` are continuous ring homomorphisms, so
postcomposition with them gives morphisms of condensed rings.

## Main definitions

* `Profinite.padicIntCondensedRing`, `Profinite.padicCondensedRing`: the condensed rings
  `ℤ̲_ℓ` and `ℚ̲_ℓ`.
* `Profinite.padicSubfieldCondensedRing`, `Profinite.padicIntegersCondensedRing`: the condensed
  rings `E̲` and `𝒪̲_E`, and `Profinite.padicIntegersRingObj`: the ring object of condensed
  abelian groups underlying `𝒪̲_E`.
* `Profinite.padicIntCondensedRingToPadicCondensedRing`,
  `Profinite.padicCondensedRingToPadicSubfieldCondensedRing`,
  `Profinite.padicIntegersCondensedRingToPadicSubfieldCondensedRing` and
  `Profinite.padicIntCondensedRingToPadicIntegersCondensedRing`: the inclusions, as morphisms of
  condensed rings.
* `Profinite.padicAlgClCondensedRing`: the condensed ring `ℚ̲̄_ℓ`, and
  `Profinite.padicAlgClRingObj`: the underlying ring object of condensed abelian groups.

-/

@[expose] public section

universe u

open CategoryTheory

namespace Profinite

open AlgebraicGeometry.ProEtale

variable (ℓ : ℕ) [Fact ℓ.Prime]

/-! ### `ℤ_ℓ` and `ℚ_ℓ` -/

/-- The condensed ring `ℤ̲_ℓ`, with sections `ℤ̲_ℓ(S) = C(S, ℤ_ℓ)` for the `ℓ`-adic topology. -/
noncomputable abbrev padicIntCondensedRing :
    Sheaf (coherentTopology Profinite.{u}) RingCat.{u + 1} :=
  continuousMapRingSheaf ℤ_[ℓ]

/-- The condensed ring `ℚ̲_ℓ`, with sections `ℚ̲_ℓ(S) = C(S, ℚ_ℓ)` for the `ℓ`-adic topology. -/
noncomputable abbrev padicCondensedRing :
    Sheaf (coherentTopology Profinite.{u}) RingCat.{u + 1} :=
  continuousMapRingSheaf ℚ_[ℓ]

/-- The inclusion `ℤ̲_ℓ ⟶ ℚ̲_ℓ`, a morphism of condensed rings. -/
noncomputable def padicIntCondensedRingToPadicCondensedRing :
    padicIntCondensedRing.{u} ℓ ⟶ padicCondensedRing ℓ :=
  continuousMapRingSheafMap PadicInt.Coe.ringHom continuous_subtype_val

/-! ### Subfields `E` of `ℚ̄_ℓ` -/

section Subfield

variable (E : Type) [Field E] [Algebra E (PadicAlgCl ℓ)]

/-- The condensed ring `E̲`, for a subfield `E` of `ℚ̄_ℓ` given by an embedding `E → ℚ̄_ℓ`, with
sections `E̲(S) = C(S, E)` for the topology of `E` restricted from `ℚ̄_ℓ`. -/
noncomputable abbrev padicSubfieldCondensedRing :
    Sheaf (coherentTopology Profinite.{u}) RingCat.{u + 1} :=
  letI := PadicAlgCl.normedFieldOfAlgebra ℓ E
  continuousMapRingSheaf E

variable [Algebra ℚ_[ℓ] E] [IsScalarTower ℚ_[ℓ] E (PadicAlgCl ℓ)]

/-- The inclusion `ℚ̲_ℓ ⟶ E̲`, a morphism of condensed rings. -/
noncomputable def padicCondensedRingToPadicSubfieldCondensedRing :
    padicCondensedRing.{u} ℓ ⟶ padicSubfieldCondensedRing ℓ E :=
  letI := PadicAlgCl.normedFieldOfAlgebra ℓ E
  continuousMapRingSheafMap (algebraMap ℚ_[ℓ] E) (continuous_algebraMap_padic_of_algebra ℓ E)

end Subfield

/-! ### The rings of integers `𝒪_E` -/

section Integers

variable (E : Type) [Field E] [Algebra E (PadicAlgCl ℓ)]

/-- The condensed ring `𝒪̲_E`, for a subfield `E` of `ℚ̄_ℓ` given by an embedding `E → ℚ̄_ℓ`,
with sections `𝒪̲_E(S) = C(S, 𝒪_E)` into the ring of integers `𝒪_E = PadicAlgCl.integers ℓ E`,
for the topology restricted from `ℚ̄_ℓ`. -/
noncomputable abbrev padicIntegersCondensedRing :
    Sheaf (coherentTopology Profinite.{u}) RingCat.{u + 1} :=
  letI := PadicAlgCl.normedFieldOfAlgebra ℓ E
  continuousMapRingSheaf (PadicAlgCl.integers ℓ E)

/-- The inclusion `𝒪̲_E ⟶ E̲`, a morphism of condensed rings. -/
noncomputable def padicIntegersCondensedRingToPadicSubfieldCondensedRing :
    padicIntegersCondensedRing.{u} ℓ E ⟶ padicSubfieldCondensedRing ℓ E :=
  letI := PadicAlgCl.normedFieldOfAlgebra ℓ E
  continuousMapRingSheafMap (PadicAlgCl.integers ℓ E).subtype continuous_subtype_val

/-- The condensed ring `𝒪̲_E` as a ring object of condensed abelian groups: the abelian sheaf
underlying `padicIntegersCondensedRing ℓ E`, with the ring object structure
`CategoryTheory.Sheaf.monObj_sheafCompose_obj`. -/
noncomputable abbrev padicIntegersRingObj : Sheaf (coherentTopology Profinite.{u}) Ab.{u + 1} :=
  (sheafCompose (coherentTopology Profinite.{u}) (forget₂ RingCat.{u + 1} Ab.{u + 1})).obj
    (padicIntegersCondensedRing ℓ E)

/-- The condensed ring `𝒪̲_E` is a commutative ring object of condensed abelian groups. -/
instance isCommMonObj_padicIntegersRingObj : IsCommMonObj (padicIntegersRingObj.{u} ℓ E) :=
  letI := PadicAlgCl.normedFieldOfAlgebra ℓ E
  isCommMonObj_continuousMapRingObj (PadicAlgCl.integers ℓ E)

variable [Algebra ℚ_[ℓ] E] [IsScalarTower ℚ_[ℓ] E (PadicAlgCl ℓ)]

/-- The inclusion `ℤ̲_ℓ ⟶ 𝒪̲_E`, a morphism of condensed rings. -/
noncomputable def padicIntCondensedRingToPadicIntegersCondensedRing :
    padicIntCondensedRing.{u} ℓ ⟶ padicIntegersCondensedRing ℓ E :=
  letI := PadicAlgCl.normedFieldOfAlgebra ℓ E
  continuousMapRingSheafMap (padicIntToIntegers ℓ E) (continuous_padicIntToIntegers ℓ E)

end Integers

/-! ### `ℚ̄_ℓ` -/

section PadicAlgCl

/-- The subfunctor of `S ↦ C(S, ℚ̄_ℓ)` on profinite sets of the continuous maps with values in a
finite subextension `E ⊆ ℚ̄_ℓ` of `ℚ_ℓ`: a section is a continuous map into one finite local
field `E`, regarded in `ℚ̄_ℓ`. -/
def padicAlgClSubfunctor :
    Subfunctor (ContinuousMap.yonedaPresheaf Profinite.toTopCat.{u} (PadicAlgCl ℓ)) where
  obj S := {a | a ∈ PadicAlgCl.finiteIntermediateFieldValued ℓ S.unop}
  map {S S'} u a ha := by
    obtain ⟨E, hE, h⟩ := ha
    exact ⟨E, hE, fun x ↦ h _⟩

/-- The sections with values in a finite subextension form a sheaf for the coherent topology:
a covering is refined by a finite jointly surjective family `{Sᵢ ⟶ S}`, and if `a ∘ πᵢ` takes
values in the finite subextension `Eᵢ` for each `i`, then `a` takes values in their compositum,
which is again finite. -/
lemma isSheaf_padicAlgClSubfunctor :
    Presieve.IsSheaf (coherentTopology Profinite.{u}) (padicAlgClSubfunctor.{u} ℓ).toFunctor := by
  rw [Subfunctor.isSheaf_iff _
    (h := (isSheaf_iff_isSheaf_of_type _ _).mp (isSheaf_yonedaPresheaf (PadicAlgCl ℓ)))]
  intro S a ha
  obtain ⟨α, _, Y, π, hπ, hmem⟩ := (coherentTopology.mem_sieves_iff_hasEffectiveEpiFamily _).mp ha
  choose E hE h using hmem
  have hsurj : ∀ b : S.unop, ∃ (i : α) (y : Y i), π i y = b :=
    ((Profinite.effectiveEpiFamily_tfae Y π).out 0 2).mp hπ
  refine ⟨⨆ i, E i, inferInstance, fun x ↦ ?_⟩
  obtain ⟨i, y, rfl⟩ := hsurj x
  exact (le_iSup E i) (h i y)

/-- The presheaf of rings `S ↦ {a ∈ C(S, ℚ̄_ℓ) | a takes values in a finite subextension}` on
profinite sets, lifted to `Type (u + 1)`. -/
noncomputable def padicAlgClRingPresheaf : Profinite.{u}ᵒᵖ ⥤ RingCat.{u + 1} where
  obj S := RingCat.of (ULift.{u + 1} (PadicAlgCl.finiteIntermediateFieldValued ℓ S.unop))
  map u := RingCat.ofHom
    (ULift.ringEquiv.symm.toRingHom.comp
      (((((ContinuousMap.compRightAlgHom ℤ (PadicAlgCl ℓ) u.unop.hom.hom).toRingHom).comp
          (PadicAlgCl.finiteIntermediateFieldValued ℓ _).subtype).codRestrict
          (PadicAlgCl.finiteIntermediateFieldValued ℓ _) (fun a ↦ by
            obtain ⟨E, hE, h⟩ := a.2
            exact ⟨E, hE, fun x ↦ h _⟩)).comp ULift.ringEquiv.toRingHom))
  map_id _ := rfl
  map_comp _ _ := rfl

/-- The presheaf of types underlying `padicAlgClRingPresheaf ℓ` is the subfunctor
`padicAlgClSubfunctor ℓ`, lifted by one universe. -/
noncomputable def padicAlgClRingPresheafForgetIso :
    padicAlgClRingPresheaf.{u} ℓ ⋙ forget RingCat.{u + 1} ≅
      (padicAlgClSubfunctor.{u} ℓ).toFunctor ⋙ uliftFunctor.{u + 1} :=
  NatIso.ofComponents (fun _ ↦ Iso.refl _) (fun _ ↦ rfl)

/-- The presheaf of rings of sections with values in a finite subextension is a sheaf. -/
lemma isSheaf_padicAlgClRingPresheaf :
    Presheaf.IsSheaf (coherentTopology Profinite.{u}) (padicAlgClRingPresheaf ℓ) := by
  rw [Presheaf.isSheaf_iff_isSheaf_forget _ _ (forget RingCat.{u + 1}),
    Presheaf.isSheaf_of_iso_iff (padicAlgClRingPresheafForgetIso ℓ),
    isSheaf_iff_isSheaf_of_type, Presieve.isSheaf_comp_uliftFunctor_iff]
  exact isSheaf_padicAlgClSubfunctor ℓ

/-- The condensed ring `ℚ̲̄_ℓ`, following Definition 3.6 of
`WeilConjectures/RefactorInstruction.tex`: a section over a profinite set `S` is a continuous map
`S → E` into a finite subextension `E ⊆ ℚ̄_ℓ` of `ℚ_ℓ`, regarded in `ℚ̄_ℓ`, so that two such
maps are identified when they agree pointwise in `ℚ̄_ℓ`. Restriction is precomposition and the
ring operations are computed in a common finite subextension. It is *not* `C(-, ℚ̄_ℓ)` for the
valuation topology of `ℚ̄_ℓ`, which has more sections. -/
noncomputable def padicAlgClCondensedRing :
    Sheaf (coherentTopology Profinite.{u}) RingCat.{u + 1} :=
  ⟨padicAlgClRingPresheaf ℓ, isSheaf_padicAlgClRingPresheaf ℓ⟩

/-- The condensed ring `ℚ̲̄_ℓ` as a ring object of condensed abelian groups: the abelian sheaf
underlying `padicAlgClCondensedRing ℓ`, with the ring object structure
`CategoryTheory.Sheaf.monObj_sheafCompose_obj`. -/
noncomputable abbrev padicAlgClRingObj : Sheaf (coherentTopology Profinite.{u}) Ab.{u + 1} :=
  (sheafCompose (coherentTopology Profinite.{u}) (forget₂ RingCat.{u + 1} Ab.{u + 1})).obj
    (padicAlgClCondensedRing ℓ)

/-- The condensed ring `ℚ̲̄_ℓ` is a commutative ring object of condensed abelian groups. -/
instance isCommMonObj_padicAlgClRingObj : IsCommMonObj (padicAlgClRingObj.{u} ℓ) :=
  Sheaf.isCommMonObj_sheafCompose_obj (coherentTopology Profinite.{u})
    (padicAlgClCondensedRing ℓ) fun S
      (a b : ULift.{u + 1} (PadicAlgCl.finiteIntermediateFieldValued ℓ S.unop)) ↦ mul_comm a b

end PadicAlgCl

end Profinite
```

</details>

<summary>WeilConjectures/SixFunctors/ProEtale/ModuleObject/BaseChangeTheorems/Proper.lean</summary>

```lean
/-

Released under Apache 2.0 license as described in the file LICENSE.
Authors: Nailin Guan

module

public import WeilConjectures.CategoryTheory.RingObjects.Condensed.EllAdic
public import WeilConjectures.SixFunctors.ProEtale.ModuleObject.BaseChangeTheorems.MorphismDefs

/-!

# The proper base change theorem for constructible `E`- and `ℚ̄_ℓ`-sheaves

Let `ℓ` be a prime and `E` a finite extension of `ℚ_ℓ`, with its normed field structure. Its
condensed ring is `E̲ = C(-, E)` (`Profinite.continuousMapRingObj E`, of
`WeilConjectures.CategoryTheory.RingObjects.Condensed.ContinuousMap`), and the coefficient ring
on `X_proét` is `E_X = p_X^* E̲`. Fix a cartesian square of schemes

```
X' --g'--> X
|          |
f'         f
v          v
Y' --g---> Y
```

with `f` **proper**, recorded as `sq : IsPullback g' f' f g`. This file states the proper base
change theorem for bounded constructible `E`-sheaves: the base-change morphism
`BC_sq : Rf_* ⋙ Lg^* ⟶ Lg'^* ⋙ Rf'_*` of
`WeilConjectures.SixFunctors.ProEtale.ModuleObject.BaseChangeTheorems.MorphismDefs` is an
isomorphism of functors `Dᵇc(X)⟦E̲⟧ ⥤ Dᵇc(Y')⟦E̲⟧`. No hypothesis is imposed on `g`. The same
holds for the condensed ring `ℚ̲̄_ℓ` of
`WeilConjectures.CategoryTheory.RingObjects.Condensed.EllAdic`
(`Profinite.padicAlgClRingObj`), whose sections are continuous maps into finite
subextensions of `ℚ̄_ℓ`.

The proof reduces to the torsion case, the étale proper base change theorem
`AlgebraicGeometry.Etale.isIso_baseChange_of_isProper`, by writing a constructible
`𝒪_E`-complex as the derived limit of its reductions modulo `ϖⁿ` and inverting `ℓ`
(Bhatt–Scholze, Section 6.7); the `ℚ̄_ℓ` case follows by passing to the colimit over the finite
subextensions `E`. Both are currently `sorry`.

As for the bounded constructible six functors
(`WeilConjectures.SixFunctors.ProEtale.ModuleObject.Derived.BoundedConstructible`), all schemes are
separated and of finite type over a field `k` of characteristic `p > 0` with `p ≠ ℓ`, and all
morphisms are morphisms over `Spec k`.

## Main statements

- `ProEtale.CondensedRing.isIso_boundedConstructibleBaseChange_of_isProper`: proper base
  change on bounded constructible `E`-sheaves.
- `ProEtale.CondensedRing.isIso_boundedConstructibleBaseChange_of_isProper_padicAlgCl`: proper
  base change on bounded constructible `ℚ̄_ℓ`-sheaves.

## References

- Bhatt–Scholze, *The pro-étale topology for schemes*, Section 6.7.
- Stacks Project, Section 59.91, *The proper base change theorem*,
  <https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/095S>

-/

@[expose] public section

universe u

open CategoryTheory Limits

namespace AlgebraicGeometry.ProEtale.CondensedRing

open Scheme

variable (ℓ : ℕ) [Fact ℓ.Prime]
  (E : Type) [NormedField E] [NormedAlgebra ℚ_[ℓ] E] [FiniteDimensional ℚ_[ℓ] E]
  (k : Type u) [Field k] [Fact (ringChar k).Prime] [Fact (ringChar k ≠ ℓ)]
  {X Y X' Y' : Scheme.{u}} [X.Over (Spec (.of k))] [Y.Over (Spec (.of k))]
  [X'.Over (Spec (.of k))] [Y'.Over (Spec (.of k))]
  [LocallyOfFiniteType (X ↘ Spec (.of k))] [QuasiCompact (X ↘ Spec (.of k))]
  [IsSeparated (X ↘ Spec (.of k))]
  [LocallyOfFiniteType (Y ↘ Spec (.of k))] [QuasiCompact (Y ↘ Spec (.of k))]
  [IsSeparated (Y ↘ Spec (.of k))]
  [LocallyOfFiniteType (X' ↘ Spec (.of k))] [QuasiCompact (X' ↘ Spec (.of k))]
  [IsSeparated (X' ↘ Spec (.of k))]
  [LocallyOfFiniteType (Y' ↘ Spec (.of k))] [QuasiCompact (Y' ↘ Spec (.of k))]
  [IsSeparated (Y' ↘ Spec (.of k))]
  {f : X ⟶ Y} {g : Y' ⟶ Y} {f' : X' ⟶ Y'} {g' : X' ⟶ X} [f.IsOver (Spec (.of k))]
  [g.IsOver (Spec (.of k))] [f'.IsOver (Spec (.of k))] [g'.IsOver (Spec (.of k))]

include ℓ k in
/-- **The proper base change theorem** for bounded constructible `E`-sheaves, `E` a finite
extension of `ℚ_ℓ`: for a cartesian square of schemes

```
X' --g'--> X
|          |
f'         f
v          v
Y' --g---> Y
```

with `f` proper, the base-change morphism `BC_sq : Rf_* ⋙ Lg^* ⟶ Lg'^* ⋙ Rf'_*` is an
isomorphism of functors `Dᵇc(X)⟦E̲⟧ ⥤ Dᵇc(Y')⟦E̲⟧`. -/
theorem isIso_boundedConstructibleBaseChange_of_isProper [IsProper f]
    (sq : IsPullback g' f' f g) :
    IsIso (boundedConstructibleBaseChange (Profinite.continuousMapRingObj E) ℓ k sq.toCommSq) :=
  sorry

/-- **The proper base change theorem** for bounded constructible `ℚ̄_ℓ`-sheaves: for a cartesian
square of schemes `sq : IsPullback g' f' f g` with `f` proper, the base-change morphism
`BC_sq : Rf_* ⋙ Lg^* ⟶ Lg'^* ⋙ Rf'_*` is an isomorphism of functors
`Dᵇc(X)⟦ℚ̲̄_ℓ⟧ ⥤ Dᵇc(Y')⟦ℚ̲̄_ℓ⟧`, for the condensed ring
`ℚ̲̄_ℓ = Profinite.padicAlgClRingObj ℓ`. -/
theorem isIso_boundedConstructibleBaseChange_of_isProper_padicAlgCl [IsProper f]
    (sq : IsPullback g' f' f g) :
    IsIso (boundedConstructibleBaseChange
      (Profinite.padicAlgClRingObj ℓ) ℓ k sq.toCommSq) :=
  sorry

end AlgebraicGeometry.ProEtale.CondensedRing
```

**thm-pod-finite-reduction**

Use the smooth Poincare setting, with
the admissible structure morphism and normalized smooth orientation.
Let $n\geq m\geq1$, put $A_n=\mathcal O_E/\varpi_E^n$ and
$A_m=\mathcal O_E/\varpi_E^m$, and let $K_n$ be a
site-perfect constructible
$A_n$-complex. Put $K_m=K_n\otimes^L_{A_n}A_m$.
The coefficient comparisons identify the reduction of the
derived Poincare pairing
for $K_n$ with the pairing for $K_m$. In particular, writing
$K_n^\vee$ for its coefficient dual,
the canonical isomorphisms make the square

$$
\begin{array}{ccc}
R\Gamma_c(X,K_n)^\vee\otimes^L_{A_n}A_m
&\xrightarrow{\beta_{K_n}^{\sharp,-1}\otimes1}&
R\Gamma(X,K_n^\vee(d))[2d]\otimes^L_{A_n}A_m\\
\downarrow\wr&&\downarrow\wr\\
R\Gamma_c(X,K_m)^\vee
&\xrightarrow{\beta_{K_m}^{\sharp,-1}}&
R\Gamma(X,K_m^\vee(d))[2d]
\end{array}
$$

commute. Here $\beta^{\sharp,-1}$ denotes the inverse of the
tensor–Hom adjoint of the pairing.

**thm-pod-identification-of-the-comparison**

Fix $\Lambda\in\{A_{E,n},\mathcal O_E,E,\Qlb\}$, the smooth
geometrically connected $k$-scheme $X=X_0\times_{k_0}k$ of dimension $d$
(not necessarily proper), and its
structure morphism $a_X:X\to s=\operatorname{Spec}k$ as in
the Poincare duality conventions.
Let $K\in D^b_{ctf}(X,\Lambda)$ be
perfect over $\Lambda_X$, with
coefficient dual $K^\vee$.
The tensor–internal-Hom adjoint of the
derived Poincare pairing is
the canonical isomorphism

$$
b_K^\sharp:R\Gamma(X,K^\vee(d))[2d]
\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }R\operatorname{Hom}_\Lambda(R\Gamma_c(X,K),\Lambda).
$$

Its inverse is the global Verdier-duality isomorphism followed by the
smooth-purity identification of the Verdier dual.

**thm-pod-global-verdier-duality**

Use the Poincare duality conventions of [def-pod-conventions] (so $X_0$
is smooth, separated of finite type and geometrically connected of pure
dimension $d$, not necessarily proper). The statement below also
holds verbatim for a smooth, proper, geometrically connected $k$-scheme
$X$ of pure dimension $d$ not assumed to descend to $k_0$ (see
[thm-sf-global-duality]). Let $K\in D^b_{ctf}(X,\Lambda)$. For the admissible structure
morphism $a_X:X\to s$ there is a canonical isomorphism

$$
R\Gamma_c(X,K)^\vee\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma(X,\mathbb D_XK),
$$

with the cohomology functors
of the fixed coefficient stage. It is natural in $K$ and compatible
with shifts. For a site-perfect
$K$, it is also compatible with finite coefficient reduction and
scalar extension under the smooth orientation and its coefficient
comparisons. At $K=\Lambda_X$ it identifies
$R\Gamma_c(X,\Lambda_X)^\vee$ with $R\Gamma(X,\omega_{X,\Lambda})$.

**thm-pod-point-perfectness**

Fix the finite-field datum and the coefficient stages
$\Lambda\in\{A_{E,n},\mathcal O_E,E,\Qlb\}$ of the Poincare duality
conventions [def-pod-conventions], but let $X_0$ be an arbitrary
separated finite-type $k_0$-scheme: neither properness nor smoothness
of $X_0$ is assumed. Put $X=X_0\times_{k_0}k$, let
$a_X:X\to s=\Spec k$ be the structure morphism, and let
$d\geq\dim X$. Let $K\in D^b_{ctf}(X,\Lambda )$. Then the four
complexes

$$
R\Gamma (X,K),\qquad R\Gamma _c(X,K),\qquad R\Gamma (X,K)^\vee,\qquad R\Gamma _c(X,K)^\vee
$$

are perfect point complexes at every coefficient stage, in the
following sense.

1. At finite level $\Lambda=A=A_{E,n}$ they are bounded with finite
cohomology modules and finite Tor amplitude, i.e. quasi-isomorphic to
bounded complexes of finite free $A$-modules
([def-pod-perfect-point-complex]). If $K$ has Tor amplitude in
$[a,b]$, then $R\Gamma(X,K)$ and $R\Gamma_c(X,K)$ have Tor amplitude
in $[a,b+2d]$ and their duals in $[-b-2d,-a]$.
1. At the stage $\mathcal O_E$ they lie in
$D^b_{\mathrm{fg}}(\mathcal O_E)$ and are perfect; their reductions
modulo $\varpi_E^n$ are the corresponding finite-level complexes of the
reductions $K_n=K\otimes^L_{\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n}$, and these are
perfect with one common amplitude independent of $n$.
1. At the stages $E$ and $\Qlb$ they are bounded with
finite-dimensional cohomology.

If $X_0$ is proper, the canonical comparison
$R\Gamma_c(X,K)\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma(X,K)$ of
[def-weil-ordinary-compact-cohomology] identifies the two cases; in
particular this applies in the fixed setting of
[def-pod-conventions]. (Source: the finiteness theorems of etale
cohomology, Milne, Lectures on Etale Cohomology, Sections 19 and 29,
for the finiteness and the perfectness at the point. The finite-level
compact-support membership $R\Gamma _c(X,K)\in D^b_{\mathrm{fin}}(A)$
is recorded in [prop-trf-compact-support-perfectness].)

**Scope of the external batch-5 assumption (for comparison, not a new use in this proof)**

Cited assumption of batch 5: upstream master WeilConjectures/PoincareDuality/Poincare.lean (d838516ef, #1448), declaration AlgebraicGeometry.ProEtale.CondensedRing.isIso_poincareMap, together with the other declarations of that file (uncurry_poincareMap, boundedConstructible_tateTwistDerived). Statement, as in [nt-target-poincare-isiso]: Let l be a prime and k a field of characteristic p>0 with p != l. Let X, S be separated schemes of finite type over Spec k (objects of Over (Spec k)) and p:X->S a morphism over k whose underlying morphism of schemes is smooth of pure relative dimension d (d in N). Write Qlb for the condensed ring Profinite.padicAlgClRingObj l and D^b_c(T)[[Qlb]] for the bounded constructible derived category of Qlb-module objects on the pro-etale site of T, a monoidal closed full subcategory of the ambient derived category (isMonoidal_boundedConstructible, isMonoidalClosed_boundedConstructible), with unit Qlb_T and internal hom RHom. On it Lp^* -| Rp_* (boundedConstructibleUpperStarLowerStarAdjunction) and Rp_! -| Rp^! (boundedConstructibleUpperShriekAdjunction, Rp^! = boundedConstructibleUpperShriek built from a Nagata compactification), and PF^!: Rp_! K (x) B -> Rp_!(K (x) Lp^* B) is the derived exceptional projection morphism (boundedConstructibleExceptionalProjection). Qlb_X(d)[2d] = boundedConstructibleTateTwist l X d (2d) is the shift by 2d of the Tate twist tateTwistDerived X l d [def-pur-condensed-tate-twists], bounded constructible by [nt-target-poincare-tate-bc], and pur_p: Rp^! Qlb_S ~= Qlb_X(d)[2d] (boundedConstructibleSmoothPurityIso) is the preimage under the fully faithful inclusion D^b_c -> D of the chosen smooth purity isomorphism smoothPurityIso l p d [nt-target-smooth-purity-qbar]. The Poincare duality morphism PD_p: Rp_* Qlb_X(d)[2d] -> RHom(Rp_! Qlb_X, Qlb_S) (poincareMap) is the composite of Rp_*(pur_p^{-1}), Rp_* of the identification Rp^! Qlb_S -> RHom(Qlb_X, Rp^! Qlb_S) (transpose of the left unitor) and the Verdier exchange morphism Phi_{p,K,L}: Rp_* RHom(K, Rp^! L) -> RHom(Rp_! K, L) at K=Qlb_X, L=Qlb_S, where Phi is the transpose of the pairing Rp_! K (x) Rp_* RHom(K,Rp^! L) -> Rp_!(K (x) Lp^* Rp_* RHom(K,Rp^! L)) -> Rp_!(K (x) RHom(K,Rp^! L)) -> Rp_! Rp^! L -> L (PF^!, the counit of Lp^* -| Rp_*, evaluation, the counit of Rp_! -| Rp^!). The trace Tr_p: Rp_! Qlb_X(d)[2d] -> Qlb_S (trace) is Rp_!(pur_p^{-1}) followed by the counit of Rp_! -| Rp^!, and the compact-support cup product cup_c: Rp_! Qlb_X (x) Rp_* Qlb_X(d)[2d] -> Rp_! Qlb_X(d)[2d] (cupProduct) is PF^!, Rp_!(Qlb_X (x) counit of Lp^* -| Rp_*) and the left unitor. Then PD_p is an isomorphism in D^b_c(S)[[Qlb]] (Poincare duality). Upstream deduces it from isIso_verdierExchange of WeilConjectures.PoincareDuality.Verdier (Verdier duality: Phi_{p,K,L} is an isomorphism for all bounded constructible K, L, from the derived projection formula isIso_boundedConstructibleExceptionalProjection_app_app by the Yoneda argument Verdier.isIso_exchange_of_isIso), since the other two factors of PD_p are isomorphisms. The upstream statement does not use the smoothness instance (omitted), but PD_p is built from pur_p, whose definition needs it. Scope (what this assumption does not give, as stated in the upstream file): the perfect pairing H^i_c(X,Qlb) x H^{2d-i}(X,Qlb(d)) -> Qlb on cohomology groups over S = Spec kbar (which needs the perfectness of Rp_! Qlb_X), Frobenius equivariance, and the compatibilities with base change, compactification and composition.

```json
  "step": 1,
  "node": "def-weil-proper-morphism",
  "quote": "Let $f:X\\to S$ be a morphism of schemes. $f$ is *separated* if its diagonal $\\Delta_f:X\\to X\\times_SX$ (the morphism to the fibre product [def-pc-base-change] with both components the identity) is a closed immersion, i.e. identifies $X$ with a closed subscheme of $X\\times_SX$. $f$ is *universally closed* if for every morphism $S'\\to S$ the base change $X\\times_SS'\\to S'$ is a closed map of the underlying topological spaces. $f$ is *proper* if it is separated, of finite type [def-weil-finite-type-morphism], and universally closed. A scheme over a field $K$ is *proper over $K$* if its structure morphism to $\\operatorname{Spec}K$ is proper."
 },
 {
  "step": 2,
  "node": "lem-pur-integral-operations-preserve-constructible",
  "quote": "Fix a prime $\\ell$, a finite extension $E/\\mathbf Q_\\ell$ with uniformizer\n$\\varpi_E$, $A_{E,n}=\\mathcal O_E/\\varpi_E^n$, and a morphism $f:X\\to Y$ of\nschemes on which $\\ell$ is invertible. Let $D^b_c(W,\\mathcal O_E)$ ($W\\in\\{X,Y\\}$)\nbe the derived-complete integral constructible categories, and let $Lf^*$ and\n$\\widehat\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}$ be the integral derived pullback and\ncompleted tensor product. Then:\n\n1. for $K\\in D^b_c(Y,\\mathcal O_E)$ one has $Lf^*K\\in D^b_c(X,\\mathcal O_E)$,\nand for every $n$ the canonical map gives\n$(Lf^*K)\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n}\\cong f^{-1}(K\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n})$;\n2. for $M,N\\in D^b_c(W,\\mathcal O_E)$ one has\n$M\\widehat\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}N\\in D^b_c(W,\\mathcal O_E)$, and\n$(M\\widehat\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}N)\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n}\\cong\n(M\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n})\\otimes^L_{A_{E,n}}(N\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n})$\nfor every $n$."
 },
 {
  "step": 2,
  "node": "lem-weil-pbc-integral-stratified-perfect-characterization",
  "quote": "These conditions also describe exactly the category of [def-weil-derived-complete-integral-detailed] whenever that notation is used over such a $T$, with the same completed ring sheaf and reduction functors. This full subcategory is closed under shifts, cones, standard truncations and cohomology sheaves placed in degree zero."
 },
 {
  "step": 2,
  "node": "thm-sf-ctf-stability",
  "quote": "For derived-complete\nintegral objects, $D^b_c(X,\\mathcal O_E)=D^b_{ctf}(X,\\mathcal O_E)$.\nGeneral internal Hom and the exceptional adjoint preserve this\ncategory; Verdier duality is an anti-equivalence on it, with the\ncanonical finite reductions of these operations. These conclusions\npass to the bounded constructible categories at $E$ and\n$\\Qlb$, with their specified coefficient-passage operations.\nThe elementary site-perfect formula in (ii) retains its stated\nhypothesis. No unrestricted unbounded pro-etale exceptional\nadjunction is asserted here."
 },
 {
  "step": 2,
  "node": "lem-cbc-integral-compact-support-uniform-restriction",
  "quote": "Fix a prime $\\ell$, a finite extension $E/\\mathbf Q_\\ell$, its valuation ring $A=\\mathcal O_E$, a uniformizer $\\varpi$, and $I=(\\varpi)$. Use the completed coefficient sheaves $A_T=\\varprojlim_{n\\geq1}(A/I^n)_T$ and $D(T_{\\mathrm{proet}},A)=D(\\operatorname{Mod}(A_T))$ of [def-weil-pbc-adic-coefficient-datum]. Let $X,Y$ be noetherian schemes (admitting finite affine covers by spectra of Noetherian rings [def-pc-scheme, def-weil-pbc-noetherian-ring]) of finite Krull dimension [def-kun-topological-krull-dimension], with $\\ell$ invertible on both. Let $f:X\\to Y$ be separated and of finite type [def-weil-proper-morphism, def-weil-finite-type-morphism], and choose a compactification\n$$X\\xrightarrow{j}\\overline X\\xrightarrow{p}Y,\\qquad pj=f,$$\nwith $j$ a quasi-compact open immersion [def-weil-open-immersion] and $p$ proper [def-weil-proper-morphism].\nFor $T=X,Y$, let $\\mathcal C_T=\\Dbc(T_{\\mathrm{proet}},A)$ be exactly the full category of [def-weil-pbc-bounded-constructible-complete]: its objects are derived $I$-complete, their normalized reduction towers have common bounded adic-lisse finite-type local models, and they admit compatible etale-origin witnesses. Use its inherited triangulated structure [lem-weil-pbc-uniform-complete-triangulated].\nIdentify $A_{\\overline X}|_X=A_X$ by restriction to the open subscheme. Let $j_!$ be exact module extension by zero on this localized ringed site, applied degreewise [def-pur-localized-ringed-site, lem-pur-site-restriction-module-adjunction-exact], and let $Rp_*$ be the completed-coefficient derived module direct image [prop-weil-pbc-direct-image-limits]. Define $Rf_!^c=Rp_*j_!$, the chosen-compactification candidate [def-weil-pbc-projection-compactification]. Then $Rf_!^c$ restricts to an exact $A$-linear functor\n$$Rf_!^c:\\mathcal C_X\\longrightarrow\\mathcal C_Y$$\n[def-weil-linear-functor, def-weil-triangulated-category]. In particular the output belongs to the full compatible uniform category just specified, including its compatible etale-origin witnesses."
 },
 {
  "step": 2,
  "node": "lem-sf-integral-direct-image-constructible",
  "quote": "Let $K_0$ be a field, let $X,Y$ be finite-type $K_0$-schemes and $f:X\\to Y$ a $K_0$-morphism (of finite type; separatedness is not assumed) [def-weil-scheme-morphism-properties]. Fix a prime $\\ell$ invertible in $K_0$, a finite extension $E/\\Ql$ with valuation ring $\\mathcal O_E$ and uniformizer $\\varpi_E$, and put $A_{E,n}=\\mathcal O_E/\\varpi_E^n$ for $n\\geq1$ [def-weil-l-adic-coefficient-extension]. Let $K\\in D^b_c(X,\\mathcal O_E)$ be an object of the derived-complete integral category [def-weil-derived-complete-integral-detailed], put $K_n=K\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n}$, the derived reduction [def-weil-integral-derived-reduction], and let $Rf_{*,n}:D(X_{\\mathrm{proet}},A_{E,n})\\to D(Y_{\\mathrm{proet}},A_{E,n})$ and\n$$\n Rf_*K=R\\varprojlim_n\\operatorname{Res}_nRf_{*,n}K_n\\in D(Y_{\\mathrm{proet}},\\mathcal O_E)\n$$\nbe the finite-level and the integral derived direct images of [def-weil-derived-direct-image], the derived limit [def-weil-derived-limit] being taken of the inverse system with the transition maps specified there. Then $Rf_*K\\in D^b_c(Y,\\mathcal O_E)$. More precisely: $Rf_*K$ is derived complete [def-weil-derived-complete]; for every $n\\geq1$ the finite-reduction comparison $(Rf_*K)\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n}\\to Rf_{*,n}K_n$ of [def-weil-derived-direct-image] is an isomorphism; $Rf_{*,n}K_n\\in D^b_c(Y,A_{E,n})$ [def-weil-finite-torsion-constructibility]; and if the cohomological supports [def-weil-cohomological-support] of all $K_n$ lie in $[a,b]$, then those of all $Rf_{*,n}K_n$ lie in $[a,b+N]$, where $N$ is an integer (independent of $n$) such that $R^qf_*M=0$ for $q>N$ and every sheaf $M$ of $A_{E,1}$-modules on $X_{\\mathrm{et}}$ (when $K_0$ is algebraically closed, one may take $N=2\\dim X$ for nonempty $X$, with Krull dimension [def-kun-topological-krull-dimension], and $N=0$ for empty $X)."
 },
 {
  "step": 3,
  "node": "lem-coef-semilinear-derived-sections",
  "quote": "Let $\\mathcal C$ be a small site [def-weil-pbc-sites-and-cohomology] and let $B_1\\leftarrow B_2\\leftarrow\\cdots$ be a strict inverse sequence of sheaves of commutative rings on it. Let $\\mathscr S(B)$ consist of $B_n$-module sheaves $M_n$ and $B_{n+1}$-linear maps $M_{n+1}\\to\\operatorname{Res}_{n+1,n}M_n$, with compatible componentwise morphisms [def-weil-sheaf-of-modules-category, def-pc-sheaf-restrict-scalars]. Equivalently these are modules over the ring object $B_\\bullet$ in the topos of inverse sequences of sheaves on $\\mathcal C$. This is a Grothendieck abelian category with componentwise exactness. Use ordinary unbounded cochain complexes and derived categories [def-pc-cochain-complex-modules, def-weil-abstract-derived-category]. Evaluation induces exact functors $e_n:D(\\mathscr S(B))\\to D(B_n)$, computed termwise, jointly detecting isomorphisms. For $m\\geq n$, the transitions induce natural reduction maps $t_{m,n}:M_m\\otimes^L_{B_m}B_n\\to M_n$, compatible with composition through the canonical tensor associators [lem-pur-derived-change-of-rings]. An object is called normalized, or cartesian, when all these maps are isomorphisms. No flatness of the coefficient maps is assumed.\n\nLet $u:\\operatorname{Sh}(\\mathcal C')\\to\\operatorname{Sh}(\\mathcal C)$ be a morphism of topoi presented by small sites, an adjunction $u^{-1}\\dashv u_*$ whose inverse image preserves finite limits, and put $C_n=u^{-1}B_n$. Exact levelwise inverse image gives $V=u^{-1}_\\bullet:D(\\mathscr S(B))\\to D(\\mathscr S(C))$. Levelwise module direct image has a total right derived functor $G=Ru_{*,\\bullet}$ and $V\\dashv G$. There are canonical natural isomorphisms $e_nV=u^{-1}e_n$ and $e_nG\\cong Ru_{*,n}e_n$, compatible with the units, counits and transition maps. In extension form the transitions of $GN$ are $G_n(t^N_{m,n})\\circ b_{m,n,N_m}$, where $G_n=Ru_{*,n}$, $T_{m,n}=-\\otimes^L_{B_m}B_n$, $S_{m,n}=-\\otimes^L_{C_m}C_n$, and $b_{m,n}:T_{m,n}G_m\\to G_nS_{m,n}$ is the mate of the inverse of $S_{m,n}u^{-1}\\cong u^{-1}T_{m,n}$ [def-weil-pbc-adjunction-mate]. For an open localization, exact extension by zero $j_!$ and restriction act on systems and their derived categories, with the ordinary level adjunctions [lem-pur-site-restriction-module-adjunction-exact].\n\nFor a compatible map $B_\\bullet\\to D_\\bullet$ of ring systems, derived extension of scalars is defined on these ordinary derived categories and evaluates to $M_n\\otimes^L_{B_n}D_n$. Derived tensor of systems evaluates to $M_n\\otimes^L_{B_n}N_n$. These operations use K-flat resolutions and the total tensor complex [def-pc-kflat-complex, def-pc-tensor-complex-sheaves]. Let $H_B(M,N)=R\\mathcal Hom_{B_\\bullet}(M,N)$ be internal Hom on the ringed topos of systems [def-pur-derived-internal-hom]. For cartesian $M$ and arbitrary $N$, there are canonical natural identifications $e_nH_B(M,N)\\cong R\\mathcal Hom_{B_n}(M_n,N_n)$, carrying system evaluation to ordinary tensor-Hom evaluation. Its reduction transition is the transpose of\n$$S(H_m)\\otimes^L M_n\\xrightarrow{1\\otimes t_{m,n}^{M,-1}}S(H_m)\\otimes^L S(M_m)\\cong S(H_m\\otimes^L M_m)\\xrightarrow{S(\\mathrm{ev})}S(N_m)\\xrightarrow{t^N_{m,n}}N_n,$$\nwhere in this formula $S=-\\otimes^L_{B_m}B_n$ and $H_m=R\\mathcal Hom_{B_m}(M_m,N_m)$. No fiberwise-Hom formula is asserted for noncartesian first inputs.\n\nPullback-composition identifications, maps of module sheaves, the preceding tensor and evaluation maps, and units and counits give natural transformations on the ordinary derived categories of systems. Their specified exchange maps are the ordinary adjunction-mate words; this includes open-adjunction exchange and composites with proper direct-image composition. The coefficient projection comparison for $Ru_*$ is precisely $b_{m,n}$ above. The general tensor projection map is the transpose of $u^{-1}(Ru_*N\\otimes^LP)\\cong u^{-1}Ru_*N\\otimes^Lu^{-1}P\\xrightarrow{\\varepsilon_N\\otimes1}N\\otimes^Lu^{-1}P$. Evaluation, composition and taking these specified adjunction transposes agree with the levelwise constructions and ordinary mate-pasting identities. These assertions concern maps actually constructed on $D(\\mathscr S(B))$, without a claim that evaluations detect equality of arbitrary morphisms, that comparisons are invertible on arbitrary inputs, or that evaluated K-injective systems are K-injective. No right adjoint on the subcategory of normalized systems is asserted."
 },
 {
  "step": 3,
  "node": "lem-sf-semilinear-proper-open-mate-pasting",
  "quote": "Let $C_1\\leftarrow C_2\\leftarrow\\cdots$ be any inverse sequence of commutative rings, and put $B_{n,T}=(C_n)_T$ on a fixed small skeleton of the pro-etale site of every scheme $T$ [def-weil-pbc-proetale-site]. Let $\\mathscr S_T$ be the category of strict semilinear module systems: an object consists of $B_{n,T}$-module sheaves $M_n$ and $B_{n+1,T}$-linear maps $M_{n+1}\\to\\operatorname{Res}_{n+1,n}M_n$; morphisms commute with transitions. Use $D(\\mathscr S_T)$ and its simultaneous section models [lem-coef-semilinear-derived-sections]. For a scheme map $v$, let $P_v$ be exact termwise inverse image and $S_v$ total derived direct image, with $P_v\\dashv S_v$, unit $\\eta_v$ and counit $\\varepsilon_v$. For an open immersion $a$, let $J_a\\dashv P_a$ be exact termwise extension by zero, with open unit the identity on sections over the open [lem-sf-open-tensor-normalization]. Direct-image compositors are mates of inverse-image compositors [lem-weil-pbc-mate-pasting].\n\nLet $Q$ be a commutative square of schemes with top arrow $k:U\\hookrightarrow V$, bottom arrow $i:W\\hookrightarrow Z$ quasi-compact open, vertical arrows $r:U\\to W$, $p:V\\to Z$ proper, and $pk=ir$ [def-weil-scheme-morphism-properties]. Set $V_W=V\\times_ZW$ with projections $b:V_W\\to V$, $q:V_W\\to W$, and $s=(k,r):U\\to V_W$ [def-pc-fibre-product]. Then $s$ is clopen. Let $\\pi_s:M\\to J_sP_sM$ be projection to the component $s(U)$, identity there and zero on its complementary open. Put\n$$\\alpha_Q:P_pJ_i\\xrightarrow{\\beta_{i,p}}J_bP_q\\xrightarrow{J_b\\pi_sP_q}J_bJ_sP_sP_q\\cong J_kP_r,$$\nwhere $\\beta$ is the open-unit-normalized map of [lem-sf-open-tensor-normalization], and define its proper mate\n$$\\Gamma_Q=(S_pJ_k\\varepsilon_r)\\circ(S_p\\alpha_QS_r)\\circ(\\eta_pJ_iS_r):J_iS_r\\to S_pJ_k.$$\nThere is a canonical isomorphism\n$$\\lambda_Q:P_iS_pJ_k\\cong S_qP_bJ_k\\cong S_qJ_s\\cong S_qS_s\\cong S_r.$$\nThe first isomorphism is right-adjoint conjugation of the cartesian open comparison $P_pJ_i\\cong J_bP_q$; the middle identification $J_s=S_s$ is the clopen section formula. The map $\\Gamma_Q$ is the $J_i\\dashv P_i$ adjunct of $\\lambda_Q^{-1}$, and $\\lambda_Q\\circ P_i\\Gamma_Q=1$ under the open unit.\n\nFor vertical pasting $Q_1$ above $Q_2$ with vertical maps $r_1,r_2$ on the left and $p_1,p_2$ on the right, and for horizontal pasting $Q_1$ left of $Q_2$ with top opens $k,l$, bottom opens $i,j$ and verticals $r,p,q$, respectively,\n$$\\Gamma_{Q_2Q_1}=(S_{p_2}\\Gamma_{Q_1})\\circ(\\Gamma_{Q_2}S_{r_1}),\\qquad\n\\Gamma_{Q_2Q_1}=(\\Gamma_{Q_2}J_k)\\circ(J_j\\Gamma_{Q_1}).$$\nFor base change $h:Z_1\\to Z$, write $a:V_1\\to V$, $c:W_1\\to W$, $d:U_1\\to U$ for the projections, and $Q_1$ for the induced square. Let $E_p:P_hS_p\\to S_{p_1}P_a$ and $E_r:P_cS_r\\to S_{r_1}P_d$ be the canonical pullback-square mates. Then\n$$(S_{p_1}\\beta_{k,a})\\circ(E_pJ_k)\\circ(P_h\\Gamma_Q)\n=(\\Gamma_{Q_1}P_d)\\circ(J_{i_1}E_r)\\circ(\\beta_{i,h}S_r).$$\nAll equations use the canonical compositors, hold in the semilinear derived categories before evaluation, and are independent of parenthesization. Identity verticals or identity horizontals give identity transformations. They apply also to noncartesian refinement squares, where $r=1$ and $s$ is the clopen section.\n\nIf a commutative ring $B$ has compatible maps $B\\to C_n$, termwise restriction to constant-$B$ module towers identifies $\\Gamma_Q$ with the normalized constant-tower mate of [lem-cbc-strict-tower-proper-open-calculus], via the canonical scalar-restriction direct-image and pullback comparisons of [lem-weil-pbc-ring-sheaf-change-adjunction]. Evaluation gives the same mate over $C_n$. When $C_n$ is finite and the evaluated input has a bounded-below etale-origin witness [def-weil-pbc-etale-origin-witness], it is the specified finite proper/open comparison. No faithfulness of derived evaluation or scalar restriction on morphisms is asserted."
 },
 {
  "step": 3,
  "node": "lem-sf-ambient-proper-open-pasting",
  "quote": "Fix $k=\\overline{\\mathbf F}_q$, a prime $\\ell\\ne\\operatorname{char}k$, a finite extension $E/\\mathbf Q_\\ell$, its valuation ring $R$ and uniformizer $t$, and $A_n=R/t^n$ [def-weil-l-adic-coefficient-extension]. All schemes are finite-type $k$-schemes; their structure morphisms need not be separated [def-weil-scheme-morphism-properties]. Use small pro-etale sites [def-weil-pbc-proetale-site]. Choose either (a) $\\mathcal D_T=D(T_{\\mathrm{proet}},A_n)$ for one fixed $n\\ge1$, with ordinary unbounded derived module operations [def-weil-pbc-proetale-operations], or (b) $\\mathcal D_T=D(\\mathscr S_T)$, where $\\mathscr S_T$ consists of module sheaves $M_n$ over $(A_n)_T$ and semilinear transition maps $M_{n+1}\\to\\operatorname{Res}_{A_{n+1}\\to A_n}M_n$, with componentwise morphisms commuting with transitions. In (b) use the simultaneous ringed-diagram-site operations of [lem-coef-semilinear-derived-sections], not completed integral operations or derived maps chosen from their evaluations. Write $P_u\\dashv S_u$ for exact inverse image and its total right-derived direct image, and $J_j\\dashv P_j$ for exact extension by zero along an open immersion [lem-pur-site-restriction-module-adjunction-exact]. Use ordinary derived tensor over the constant ring or over the ring system, respectively.\n\nLet $d_{v,u}:P_uP_v\\cong P_{vu}$ be the canonical pullback compositor, $c_{v,u}:S_vS_u\\cong S_{vu}$ the inverse of its adjunction conjugate, and $e_{b,j}:J_bJ_j\\cong J_{bj}$ the inverse open-adjunction conjugate of $P_jP_b\\cong P_{bj}$. These are the canonical derived direct-image and open-extension compositors. They are natural, unital, associative, and identify the canonical adjunction for a composite with the composite adjunction [def-weil-pbc-composite-adjunction]. If $\\mu_u:P_u(F\\otimes G)\\cong P_uF\\otimes P_uG$ is canonical derived tensor comparison [lem-weil-pbc-ringed-topoi-derived-tensor-pullback], then, after $d$ identifies pullbacks, $\\mu_{vu}=\\mu_u\\circ P_u(\\mu_v)$, with the arguments and tensor associators inserted. This assertion also fixes the tensor/composition coherence in setting (b).\n\nFor every commutative square\n$$Q=\\begin{array}{ccc}U&\\xrightarrow{k_1}&V\\\\ r\\downarrow&&\\downarrow p\\\\ W&\\xrightarrow{i}&Z\\end{array},\\qquad pk_1=ir,$$\nwith $p,r$ proper and $k_1,i$ quasi-compact open, form $V_W=V\\times_ZW$ with projections $b:V_W\\to V$, $q:V_W\\to W$ [def-pc-fibre-product]. The induced $s=(k_1,r):U\\to V_W$ is open and closed. Let $\\operatorname{pr}_s:F\\to J_sP_sF$ be identity on $s(U)$ and zero on its complementary open. Let $\\beta:P_pJ_i\\cong J_bP_q$ be canonical open base change, normalized by the open unit as in [lem-sf-open-tensor-normalization]. Define\n$$a_Q:P_pJ_i\\xrightarrow{\\beta}J_bP_q\\xrightarrow{J_b\\operatorname{pr}_sP_q}J_bJ_sP_sP_q\\cong J_{k_1}P_r,$$\nand define its proper-adjunction mate [def-weil-pbc-adjunction-mate]\n$$\\Gamma_Q=S_p(J_{k_1}\\varepsilon_r)\\circ S_p(a_QS_r)\\circ\\eta_pJ_iS_r:J_iS_r\\longrightarrow S_pJ_{k_1}.$$\nThis is precisely the normalized proper/open map used in [lem-sf-proper-open-projection-mate], also in setting (b). No Cartesian hypothesis is imposed.\n\nFor vertically stacked squares $Q_1,Q_2$ with left proper edges $r_1,r_2$, right proper edges $p_1,p_2$ and middle open $i_1$, the intrinsic clopen construction for their outer rectangle satisfies\n$$\\Gamma_{Q_2Q_1}=(S_{p_2}\\Gamma_{Q_1})\\circ(\\Gamma_{Q_2}S_{r_1}).$$\nFor horizontally adjacent squares with top opens $k_1,k_2$, bottom opens $i_1,i_2$ and proper verticals $r,p,q$, it satisfies\n$$\\Gamma_{Q_2Q_1}=(\\Gamma_{Q_2}J_{k_1})\\circ(J_{i_2}\\Gamma_{Q_1}).$$\nInsert $c,d,e$ in these equations. Squares with identity verticals or identity horizontals give identity comparisons. The pasted $a_Q$ equals the intrinsic clopen-projection $a_Q$ for the outer square. The identities apply to refinement squares $(k_1,i,r,p)=(j_1,j_2,1,u)$ and to all intermediate squares in refining compatible compactification towers [def-weil-compactifications-refinements]. They are identities of natural transformations on the entire stated unbounded categories, with no constructibility, Tor, etale-origin or normalized-system hypothesis. In (b) they hold before restriction of scalars, finite evaluation or total limit. No invertibility of $\\Gamma_Q$ on arbitrary inputs is asserted."
 },
 {
  "step": 3,
  "node": "lem-weil-compact-support-reduction-tower-functor",
  "quote": "Let $K_0$ be a field, $X,Y$ finite-type $K_0$-schemes, and $f:X\\to Y$ a separated finite-type morphism [def-weil-scheme-morphism-properties]. Fix a prime $\\ell$ invertible in $K_0$, a finite extension $E/\\mathbf Q_\\ell$ with valuation ring $R=\\mathcal O_E$ and uniformizer $\\varpi$, and put $A_n=R/\\varpi^n$ [def-weil-l-adic-coefficient-extension]. Use the small pro-etale sites and coefficient universes of [def-weil-pbc-proetale-site, def-weil-size-convention]. For a scheme $W$ write $B_W=\\widehat{\\mathcal O}_{E,W}=\\varprojlim_n(A_n)_W$ for the completed coefficient sheaf [def-weil-completed-integral-coefficients], $\\mathcal A_W=\\operatorname{Mod}(B_W)$ [def-weil-sheaf-of-modules-category], and $D(\\mathcal A_W)=D(W_{\\mathrm{proet}},R)$ [def-weil-abstract-derived-category]. Let $\\operatorname{Tow}(\\mathcal A_Y)=\\operatorname{Fun}(\\mathbf N_{\\geq1}^{\\mathrm{op}},\\mathcal A_Y)$ be the abelian category of strict inverse sequences, with compatible levelwise maps and levelwise exactness [def-weil-inverse-system, lem-weil-tower-category-levelwise-abelian]. Choose one compactification $c=(j,p)$ of $f$ [def-weil-compactifications-refinements], and let $Rf^{c}_{!,n}=Rp_{*,n}j_!$ on $(A_n)$-module derived categories [def-compact-support-direct-image-detailed]. For $K\\in D(\\mathcal A_X)$ let $K_n=K\\otimes^L_{B_X}(A_n)_X$ [def-weil-integral-derived-reduction], and let $\\operatorname{Res}_n$ be restriction of scalars along $B_Y\\to(A_n)_Y$ [def-pc-sheaf-restrict-scalars], applied termwise to complexes; it is exact by [lem-weil-restriction-of-scalars-exact].\n\nThere is an exact $R$-linear functor lifting the composite of derived reduction, extension by zero along $j$, the separately derived proper direct image $Rp_*$, and restriction of coefficients, with the scalar action induced by the compatible constant sections $R\\to\\Gamma(W_{\\mathrm{proet}},B_W)$:\n$$\n\\mathcal T_f^c:D(\\mathcal A_X)\\longrightarrow D(\\operatorname{Tow}(\\mathcal A_Y))\n$$\n[def-weil-linear-functor, def-weil-triangulated-category]. Its $n$-th evaluation is naturally isomorphic to $\\operatorname{Res}_nRf^{c}_{!,n}K_n$, including on morphisms. Under these identifications its transition from $n+1$ to $n$ is $\\operatorname{Res}_{n+1}$ of $Rf^{c}_{!,n+1}$ applied to the quotient-induced map $K_{n+1}\\to\\operatorname{Res}^{A_{n+1}}_{A_n}K_n$, followed by the coefficient-restriction comparison of [lem-sf-compact-support-restriction-of-scalars] and $\\operatorname{Res}_{n+1}\\operatorname{Res}^{A_{n+1}}_{A_n}=\\operatorname{Res}_n$. For every $m\\geq n\\geq1$ these identifications carry the composite transition from $m$ to $n$ to the map induced by $A_m\\twoheadrightarrow A_n$ and the coefficient-restriction comparison; the identifications are natural in $K$."
 },
 {
  "step": 3,
  "node": "lem-pur-integral-operations-preserve-constructible",
  "quote": "Fix a prime $\\ell$, a finite extension $E/\\mathbf Q_\\ell$ with uniformizer\n$\\varpi_E$, $A_{E,n}=\\mathcal O_E/\\varpi_E^n$, and a morphism $f:X\\to Y$ of\nschemes on which $\\ell$ is invertible. Let $D^b_c(W,\\mathcal O_E)$ ($W\\in\\{X,Y\\}$)\nbe the derived-complete integral constructible categories, and let $Lf^*$ and\n$\\widehat\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}$ be the integral derived pullback and\ncompleted tensor product. Then:\n\n1. for $K\\in D^b_c(Y,\\mathcal O_E)$ one has $Lf^*K\\in D^b_c(X,\\mathcal O_E)$,\nand for every $n$ the canonical map gives\n$(Lf^*K)\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n}\\cong f^{-1}(K\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n})$;\n2. for $M,N\\in D^b_c(W,\\mathcal O_E)$ one has\n$M\\widehat\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}N\\in D^b_c(W,\\mathcal O_E)$, and\n$(M\\widehat\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}N)\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n}\\cong\n(M\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n})\\otimes^L_{A_{E,n}}(N\\otimes^L_{\\mathcal O_E}A_{E,n})$\nfor every $n$."
 },
 {
  "step": 3,
  "node": "lem-pur-completed-operation-tower-substitution",
  "quote": "For any finite well-typed expression formed from normalized input systems, the coefficient unit systems $(B_{W,n}[0])_n$, $T_W$, and $F_f$, the corresponding expression in completed operations on their limits is naturally identified with the limit of the whole expression in systems. Its coherent reduction is identified with that whole system by the counit, including every quotient transition. Under these identifications, the associator, action unitors and Koszul-signed symmetry of completed tensor are the limits of the corresponding canonical total-complex system maps, and the canonical composition comparison of completed pullbacks is the limit of the finite adjunction-normalized composition maps. These identifications are natural for morphisms of all the input systems and compatible with substitution of one such expression into another."
 },
 {
  "step": 4,
  "node": "lem-sf-integral-compactification-system-realization",
  "quote": "For derived-complete constructible integral inputs $K_1,\\ldots,K_s$, and\nany finite well-typed expression $V$ in $P_v,S_p,J_j$ and derived tensor\nwhose intermediate objects satisfy the bounds above, there are natural\nrealization isomorphisms\n$$c_V:\\mathcal L_W V(\\mathcal R K_1,\\ldots,\\mathcal R K_s)\n\\xrightarrow{\\sim}V_{\\mathrm{int}}(K_1,\\ldots,K_s).$$\nHere $V_{\\mathrm{int}}$ replaces system pullback and tensor by completed\npullback and completed tensor, $S_p$ by integral proper direct image, and\n$S_pJ_j$ by exactly the integral compact-support functor of\n[def-weil-integral-rational-qbar-compact-support]. The isomorphisms $c_V$\nintertwine the canonical system open maps, units, counits, pullback/tensor\nand direct-image composition maps, and proper/open interchange with the\ncorresponding integral maps, including whiskering these maps by any such\nexpression. In particular this compares nested compact-support functors\nand their compactification-composition maps, not only their objects or\nfinite reductions. The integral open maps thereby equal the localized\nringed-site maps for the completed coefficient sheaves. No lift of an\narbitrary family of derived finite-level arrows, or faithfulness of\nderived evaluation on morphisms, is asserted."
 },
 {
  "step": 4,
  "node": "lem-sf-integral-compactification-comparison-bridge",
  "quote": "Then the following is an equality of actual integral morphisms $F_gF_fK\\to F_hK$:\n$$\\alpha^{\\mathrm{cbc}}_{g,f,K}\n=d_{h,K}\\circ\\mathcal L_Z(\\beta_{\\mathcal R_XK})\\circ\\mathcal L_Z\\bigl(B_g(e_{B_f\\mathcal R_XK})\\bigr)\\circ\\mathcal L_ZB_g\\mathcal R_Y(d_{f,K}^{-1})\\circ d_{g,F_fK}^{-1}.$$\nThe equality is natural in $K$ for the supplied compatible compactification tower, including towers with an identity or empty open. It compares the specified constant-tower word normalization and the specified semilinear counit normalization before any finite evaluation; no arbitrary choice of intertwining realizations is used."
 },
 {
  "step": 4,
  "node": "lem-weil-pbc-ringed-strict-tower-homotopy-limit",
  "quote": "Let $\\mathcal C$ be a small site, $\\mathcal O$ a sheaf of commutative rings, and $\\mathcal A=\\operatorname{Mod}(\\mathcal O)$ [def-weil-sheaf-of-modules-category]. Let $\\operatorname{Tow}(\\mathcal A)=\\operatorname{Fun}(\\mathbf N_{\\geq1}^{\\mathrm{op}},\\mathcal A)$, whose objects are strict inverse sequences and whose exactness is levelwise. On its derived category [def-weil-abstract-derived-category], total right derived inverse limit is computed by taking a K-injective tower resolution and then termwise inverse limit [def-pur-kinjective-complex]. This gives an exact functor $R\\lim:D(\\operatorname{Tow}(\\mathcal A))\\to D(\\mathcal A)$. For every strict tower complex $X_\\bullet$ there is a natural distinguished triangle $R\\lim X_\\bullet\\to\\prod_n^D X_n\\xrightarrow{1-s}\\prod_n^D X_n\\to(R\\lim X_\\bullet)[1]$ [def-weil-derived-limit], where $\\prod^D$ is the product in $D(\\mathcal A)$ and the $n$-th component of $s$ is transition followed by projection to $n+1$. In particular, if for every $n$ some transition $X_m\\to X_n$, $m\\geq n$, is zero in $D(\\mathcal A)$, then $R\\lim X_\\bullet=0$. If $v:X_\\bullet\\to Y_\\bullet$ is a strict map of tower complexes whose tower of mapping cones has that property, then the canonical map $R\\lim(v)$ is an isomorphism. No exactness of countable products in $\\mathcal A$ is assumed."
 },
 {
  "step": 5,
  "node": "prop-weil-pbc-rational-transport",
  "quote": "Fix a finite extension $E/\\mathbf Q_\\ell$ with valuation ring $\\mathcal O_E$ and uniformizer $\\varpi$, and the integral constructible categories and their raw rationalizations $\\Dbc(T,E)$ of [def-weil-pbc-rational-category]. Every exact $\\mathcal O_E$-linear functor $F:\\mathcal C\\to\\mathcal D$ between these integral categories [def-weil-linear-functor, def-weil-triangulated-category] has a unique $E$-linear extension $F_E:\\mathcal C[1/\\varpi]\\to\\mathcal D[1/\\varpi]$ agreeing with $F$ on objects and integral morphisms. This extension is exact, with the localized shift comparison. For two such exact $\\mathcal O_E$-linear functors $F,G:\\mathcal C\\to\\mathcal D$, every natural transformation $\\eta:F\\Rightarrow G$ extends uniquely with components $\\eta_K\\otimes1$.\n\nIn particular, let $X,Y,X',Y'$ be schemes for which these integral constructible categories are defined. Suppose the integral operations denoted $Rf_*:\\Dbc(X,\\mathcal O_E)\\to\\Dbc(Y,\\mathcal O_E)$, $Rf'_*:\\Dbc(X',\\mathcal O_E)\\to\\Dbc(Y',\\mathcal O_E)$, $Lg^*_{\\mathrm{comp}}:\\Dbc(Y,\\mathcal O_E)\\to\\Dbc(Y',\\mathcal O_E)$ and $Lg'^*_{\\mathrm{comp}}:\\Dbc(X,\\mathcal O_E)\\to\\Dbc(X',\\mathcal O_E)$ are given exact $\\mathcal O_E$-linear functors, together with a specified integral natural exchange $\\Ex^{\\mathrm{comp}}:Lg^*_{\\mathrm{comp}}Rf_*\\Rightarrow Rf'_*Lg'^*_{\\mathrm{comp}}$. They extend to exact functors on the corresponding $\\Dbc(-,E)$, and that specified exchange extends to a natural transformation between their composites, with component $\\Ex^{\\mathrm{comp}}_K\\otimes1$. This is a transport assertion for supplied integral data, not an assertion that arbitrary scheme morphisms preserve integral constructibility."
 },
 {
  "step": 5,
  "node": "lem-weil-pbc-condensed-e-realization",
  "quote": "For every $k$-morphism $h:T\\to U$ between such schemes, the ambient condensed-module functors $Lh_c^*$ and $Rh_{c,*}$ preserve $\\mathcal C^c(E)$ and have natural comparison isomorphisms\n$$J_TLh_{\\mathrm{rat}}^*\\cong Lh_c^*J_U,\\qquad J_URh_{\\mathrm{rat},*}\\cong Rh_{c,*}J_T,$$\nwhere the rational functors are those of [prop-weil-pbc-rational-transport]. These comparisons preserve the units and counits of the pullback/direct-image adjunctions: inserting a rational unit and then applying $J$ gives the condensed unit under the displayed comparisons, and the same holds for counits. They preserve the identity and composition isomorphisms of pullbacks. Thus the comparison obtained from pullback composition and the equality of two composite scheme morphisms corresponds to the same comparison in the condensed categories. These assertions concern categories, individual functors and their adjunction and composition structures, and impose no cartesian-square or properness hypothesis."
 },
 {
  "step": 5,
  "node": "lem-weil-pbc-mate-pasting",
  "quote": "Notation of [def-weil-pbc-adjunction-mate]: for adjunctions $L_1\\dashv R_1$ ($L_1:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal B$), $L_2\\dashv R_2$ ($L_2:\\mathcal C\\to\\mathcal D$) and functors $G:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal C$, $H:\\mathcal B\\to\\mathcal D$, a *square* is a natural transformation $\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_1$ and $\\alpha^\\flat:GR_1\\to R_2H$ is its mate; conjugates $\\tau^c$ are as in [def-weil-pbc-adjunction-conjugate].\n\n1. (Pasting along $G,H$.) Let moreover $L_3\\dashv R_3$ ($L_3:\\mathcal E\\to\\mathcal F$), $G_2:\\mathcal C\\to\\mathcal E$, $H_2:\\mathcal D\\to\\mathcal F$, $\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_1$ and $\\beta:L_3G_2\\to H_2L_2$. The pasted square $\\gamma=(H_2\\alpha)\\circ(\\beta G):L_3G_2G\\to H_2HL_1$ has mate $\\gamma^\\flat=(\\beta^\\flat H)\\circ(G_2\\alpha^\\flat):G_2GR_1\\to G_2R_2H\\to R_3H_2H$.\n2. (Pasting along the adjunctions.) Let $L_1\\dashv R_1$ ($\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal B$), $L_3\\dashv R_3$ ($\\mathcal B\\to\\mathcal C$), $L_2\\dashv R_2$ ($\\mathcal D\\to\\mathcal E$), $L_4\\dashv R_4$ ($\\mathcal E\\to\\mathcal F$), $G:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal D$, $H:\\mathcal B\\to\\mathcal E$, $K:\\mathcal C\\to\\mathcal F$, $\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_1$, $\\beta:L_4H\\to KL_3$. With respect to the composite adjunctions $L_3L_1\\dashv R_1R_3$ and $L_4L_2\\dashv R_2R_4$ (unit $R_1\\eta_3L_1\\circ\\eta_1$, counit $\\varepsilon_3\\circ L_3\\varepsilon_1R_3$), the pasted square $\\delta=(\\beta L_1)\\circ(L_4\\alpha):L_4L_2G\\to KL_3L_1$ has mate $\\delta^\\flat=(R_2\\beta^\\flat)\\circ(\\alpha^\\flat R_3):GR_1R_3\\to R_2HR_3\\to R_2R_4K$.\n3. (Changing the adjunctions by a transformation.) (a) For a square $\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_1$ and a third adjunction $L_3\\dashv R_3$ with $L_3:\\mathcal C\\to\\mathcal D$ and $\\tau:L_3\\to L_2$, the square $\\alpha\\circ(\\tau G):L_3G\\to HL_1$ has mate (for $L_1\\dashv R_1$, $L_3\\dashv R_3$) equal to $(\\tau^cH)\\circ\\alpha^\\flat:GR_1\\to R_2H\\to R_3H$, $\\tau^c:R_2\\to R_3$. (b) For an adjunction $L_0\\dashv R_0$ with $L_0:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal B$, $\\sigma:L_1\\to L_0$, and a square $\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_1$, the square $(H\\sigma)\\circ\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_0$ has mate (for $L_0\\dashv R_0$, $L_2\\dashv R_2$) equal to $\\alpha^\\flat\\circ(G\\sigma^c):GR_0\\to GR_1\\to R_2H$, $\\sigma^c:R_0\\to R_1$.\n4. (Identity.) For one adjunction $L\\dashv R$ and $G,H$ identity functors, the mate of $\\mathrm{id}_L$ is $\\mathrm{id}_R$."
 },
 {
  "step": 5,
  "node": "lem-pur-compact-support-condensed-realization",
  "quote": "Let $J_T:\\mathcal I_T(R)[1/t]\\simeq\\mathcal C_E^c(T)$ be the derived coefficient-extension equivalence of [lem-weil-pbc-condensed-e-realization]. Let $Rg_{*,E}$ be the derived direct image with the coefficient action and pullback identification of [def-weil-pbc-condensed-e-module-category]. Put $P_E^c=Rg_{*,E}j_{!,E}$ on those condensed module-object categories, and use the identical composite to define $P_\\Omega^c$ for the finite-extension-valued coefficient ring $\\Omega_T^c$ of [prop-weil-pbc-qbar-condensed-two-colimit], $\\Omega=\\overline{\\mathbf Q}_\\ell$. These composites preserve the indicated bounded constructible categories. There is a natural isomorphism\n$$a_{f,E,K}:J_Y((P_RK)[1/t])\\xrightarrow{\\sim}P_E^cJ_X(K[1/t]).$$\nFor finite $E\\subset F\\subset\\Omega$ and also for $F=\\Omega$, put $T_{E,F}^T=F_T^c\\otimes^L_{E_T^c}-$ (with $F_T^c=\\Omega_T^c$ in the latter case). The canonical coefficient projection comparison is an isomorphism\n$$T_{E,F}^YP_E^c\\xrightarrow{\\sim}P_F^cT_{E,F}^X.$$\nFor finite $F/E$, write $K_S$ for completed integral scalar extension to $S=\\mathcal O_F$ [def-weil-integral-scalar-extension]. There is a canonical comparison $(P_RK)_S\\simeq P_SK_S$, compatible with $\\alpha$, $a$, and the preceding condensed projection comparison after rationalization. All these specified comparisons preserve morphisms, shifts, identities, towers of finite coefficient extensions and compactification-refinement maps, with their composition coherences. This is a comparison of compact-support functors only; it makes no assertion about their right adjoints."
 },
 {
  "step": 5,
  "node": "lem-sf-open-tensor-normalization",
  "quote": "Let $j:U\\hookrightarrow X$ be an open immersion of schemes, let\n$g:X'\\to X$, and form $U'=U\\times_X X'$ with $j':U'\\hookrightarrow X'$ and\n$g_U:U'\\to U$ [def-cbc-open-immersion-square]. On the small pro-etale sites\n[def-weil-pbc-proetale-site], let $B_X,B_{X'}$ be sheaves of commutative rings\nand $g^{-1}B_X\\to B_{X'}$ a ring map; put $B_U=j^{-1}B_X$ and\n$B_{U'}=j'^{-1}B_{X'}$, with the restricted structural ring map for $g_U$.\nWork in the ordinary unbounded derived categories of these module sheaves\n[def-weil-sheaf-of-modules-category, def-weil-abstract-derived-category].\nUse exact extension by zero $j_!,j'_!$ on the localized ringed sites\n[def-pur-localized-ringed-site, lem-pur-site-restriction-module-adjunction-exact],\ncanonical derived module pullback and tensor\n[lem-weil-pbc-ringed-topoi-derived-tensor-pullback], and the canonical\npullback-composition isomorphism $\\theta:j'^*Lg^*\\cong Lg_U^*j^*$.\n\nThe open adjunction units $u_M:M\\to j^*j_!M$ and $u'_{M'}:M'\\to j'^*j'_!M'$\nare isomorphisms, in the normalization whose section over an object factoring\nthrough the open is that same section. Extension by zero is fully faithful\non these derived categories. There are canonical natural isomorphisms\n$$\\beta_M:Lg^*j_!M\\xrightarrow{\\sim}j'_!Lg_U^*M,\\qquad\n\\gamma_{M,N}:j_!M\\otimes^L_{B_X}N\\xrightarrow{\\sim}\nj_!(M\\otimes^L_{B_U}j^*N).$$\nHere $M\\in D(B_U)$ and $N\\in D(B_X)$ are arbitrary. The inverse of $\\beta_M$\nis the $j'_!\\dashv j'^*$-adjunct of $Lg_U^*u_M$, followed by $\\theta^{-1}$;\nthe inverse of $\\gamma_{M,N}$ is the $j_!\\dashv j^*$-adjunct of\n$u_M\\otimes1$ under the canonical restriction/tensor comparison. After the\nopen unit identifications, $j'^*\\beta_M$ and $j^*\\gamma_{M,N}$ are the\nidentity maps under these specified pullback and tensor identifications.\nFor constant finite $B_X=B_{X'}=A$, with the canonical constant-section structural ring map, these are precisely the canonical maps of\n[lem-cbc-proetale-extension-zero-base-change] and\n[lem-cbc-extension-zero-projection-formula]. These assertions also hold for the ringed diagram topoi of arbitrary strict\ninverse coefficient systems. More explicitly, use the site\n$\\mathbf N\\times T_{\\mathrm{proet}}$ with topology generated by vertical\npro-etale covering families [lem-weil-pbc-diagram-site-realization]. A sheaf\nof commutative rings on this site is an inverse system $B_{0,T}\\leftarrow\nB_{1,T}\\leftarrow\\cdots$; its module sheaves are the strict semilinear\nmodule systems. Use their ordinary unbounded derived category, termwise\nopen extension, and total left derived system pullback and tensor.\nThe coefficient transitions and the structural ring maps are arbitrary;\nno flatness or completion is imposed."
 },
 {
  "step": 5,
  "node": "lem-zl-compact-support-realization-factor-normalization",
  "quote": "Then $q_c$ is invertible and its inverse is the normalized single-compactification integral realization of the proper/open word: expressing integral operations in the $P_R^0$ coordinates through $q_c^{-1}$ gives the actual separately derived proper-image and open-extension units, counits, proper/open interchange and composition maps of [lem-sf-integral-compactification-system-realization, lem-sf-integral-compactification-comparison-bridge]. This fixes the integral coordinates used in the following two equalities.\n\nThe specified comparison $a_{f,E,K}$ of [lem-pur-compact-support-condensed-realization] is exactly\n$$a_{f,E,K}=Rg_{*,E}(\\delta^J_{j,K})\\circ\\chi^J_{g,j_{!,B}K}\\circ J_Y(q_{c,K}^{-1}[1/t]).$$\nHere $\\chi^J_g:J_YRg_{*,B}\\xrightarrow{\\sim}Rg_{*,E}J_Z$ is the direct-image comparison of $J$, after rationalization; $\\delta^J_j:J_Zj_{!,B}\\xrightarrow{\\sim}j_{!,E}J_X$ is the scalar/open comparison whose restriction to the open is the identity under the balanced tensor identifications, equivalently whose inverse is the open-adjunction adjunct of the open unit [lem-sf-open-tensor-normalization].\n\n"
 },
 {
```
