(b) For an adjunction $L_0\\dashv R_0$ with $L_0:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal B$, $\\sigma:L_1\\to L_0$, and a square $\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_1$, the square $(H\\sigma)\\circ\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_0$ has mate (for $L_0\\dashv R_0$, $L_2\\dashv R_2$) equal to $\\alpha^\\flat\\circ(G\\sigma^c):GR_0\\to GR_1\\to R_2H$, $\\sigma^c:R_0\\to R_1$.\n4. (Identity.) For one adjunction $L\\dashv R$ and $G,H$ identity functors, the mate of $\\mathrm{id}_L$ is $\\mathrm{id}_R$."
 },
 {
  "step": 6,
  "node": "lem-weil-pbc-condensed-flat-coefficient-calculus",
  "quote": "Fix a prime $\\ell$ [def-weil-l-adic-numbers], an algebraic closure $\\Omega=\\overline{\\mathbf Q}_\\ell$ [def-weil-algebraic-closure], and a scheme $T$ [def-pc-scheme] whose underlying space is noetherian (every ascending chain of open subsets stabilizes). For finite intermediate fields $E\\subseteq\\Omega$ [def-weil-finite-subextension-directed-set], put $E_T^c=p_T^*C(-,E)$ using [def-weil-pbc-condensed-e-module-category]. Put $\\Omega_T^c=p_T^*\\underline\\Omega$, where $\\underline\\Omega(S)$ consists of continuous functions on a profinite set $S$ with image in one finite intermediate field. Write $\\mathcal C_T^c(E)$ for the full bounded constructible subcategory of $D(\\operatorname{Mod}_{E_T^c})$ defined in [def-weil-pbc-condensed-e-module-category]: cohomology vanishes outside a finite interval, and each cohomology sheaf has a finite locally closed stratification on whose strata it is pro-etale locally the cokernel of a map between finite rank free coefficient modules. Define $\\mathcal C_T^c(\\Omega)$ by the identical condition with coefficient ring $\\Omega_T^c$. These are the master BoundedConstructible module-object categories, with no derived completeness condition. The adjunction below is on the ambient unbounded derived categories. For every finite $E\\subseteq F\\subseteq\\Omega$, the underlying $E_T^c$-module of $F_T^c$ is finite free of rank $[F:E]$. For $B=F_T^c$ or $B=\\Omega_T^c$, extension of scalars $B\\otimes_{E_T^c}-$ on all module sheaves is exact and left adjoint to exact restriction of scalars $U_{B/E}$. Its derived functor is computed by termwise tensor on any representing complex, preserves distinguished triangles and shifts, and has the natural adjunction $$\\operatorname{Hom}_{D(B)}(B\\otimes_{E_T^c}^L K,L)\\cong\\operatorname{Hom}_{D(E_T^c)}(K,U_{B/E}L).$$ For finite $E\\subseteq F\\subseteq G$ and also $G=\\Omega$, tensor multiplication gives natural comparisons $$G_T^c\\otimes_{F_T^c}^L(F_T^c\\otimes_{E_T^c}^L K)\\cong G_T^c\\otimes_{E_T^c}^L K.$$ Together with the unit comparison for $E=F$, these satisfy the unit and pentagon identities and the analogous identities for shifts. Under restriction to $E_T^c$-modules, tensor commutes with the coefficient colimit: if $\\Omega_T^c\\cong\\varinjlim_{F\\supseteq E}F_T^c$, then $\\varinjlim_{F\\supseteq E}(F_T^c\\otimes_{E_T^c}P)\\cong\\Omega_T^c\\otimes_{E_T^c}P$ naturally for every module sheaf $P$."
 },
 {
  "step": 6,
  "node": "lem-zl-compact-support-realization-factor-normalization",
  "quote": "For the same specified compactification, the coefficient projection comparison $\\xi_{f,E,F}:T_YP_E^c\\xrightarrow{\\sim}P_F^cT_X$ of [lem-pur-compact-support-condensed-realization] is exactly\n$$\\xi_{f,E,F,K}=Rg_{*,F}(\\delta^{E,F}_{j,K})\\circ\\pi_{g,j_{!,E}K}.$$\nThe open map $\\delta^{E,F}_j:T_Zj_{!,E}\\xrightarrow{\\sim}j_{!,F}T_X$ is again identity on the open under scalar balancing. The proper map $\\pi_{g,L}:T_YRg_{*,E}L\\to Rg_{*,F}T_ZL$ is the $Lg_F^*\\dashv Rg_{*,F}$-adjunct of the specific composite\n$$Lg_F^*T_YRg_{*,E}L\\xrightarrow{\\mu}T_ZLg_E^*Rg_{*,E}L\\xrightarrow{T_Z\\varepsilon_{g,E,L}}T_ZL,$$\nwhere $\\mu$ is canonical pullback/tensor balancing. This formula also holds for $F=\\Omega$, as an equality in the $F^c$-module derived category, and $\\pi_g$ is invertible on the stated domain. For an identity open $j$, both displayed compact-support comparisons reduce to their specified proper factors, with the identity open factor. The displayed maps are natural in $K$; no endomorphism, equality of proper-unit compact-support operators, or point realization is asserted."
 },
 {
  "step": 7,
  "node": "sk-thm-nagata-compactification",
  "quote": "Let $S$ be quasi-compact and quasi-separated. Every separated morphism\n$f:X\\to S$ of finite type has a dense compactification\n([sk-def-nagata-sketch-data])."
 },
 {
  "step": 7,
  "node": "det-nagata-refinement-common-dense",
  "quote": "Let $S$ be qcqs, let $f:X\\to S$ be separated of finite type, and let\n$c_k=(X\\xrightarrow{j_k}\\overline X_k\\xrightarrow{p_k}S)$, $k=1,2$, be\ncompactifications of $f$. Let $Z$ be the scheme-theoretic image of\n\n$$\n\\delta=(j_1,j_2):X\\longrightarrow P:=\\overline X_1\\times_S\\overline X_2 ,\n$$\n\nwith projections $q_k:Z\\to\\overline X_k$. Then $\\delta$ factors as a\nquasi-compact, scheme-theoretically dense open immersion $j:X\\to Z$ followed\nby the closed immersion $Z\\hookrightarrow P$; the structure map $Z\\to S$ is\nproper, so $c=(X\\xrightarrow{j}Z\\to S)$ is a dense compactification; and\neach $q_k$ is a refinement $c\\to c_k$ in the sense of\n[det-nagata-definition-refinement], i.e. $q_kj=j_k$ and\n$q_k^{-1}(j_k(X))=j(X)$.\n\nConsequently every finite nonempty family of compactifications of $f$ has a\ncommon dense refinement."
 },
 {
  "step": 7,
  "node": "det-sf-comp-composition-interchange",
  "quote": "Work in the standing scheme setting of [sk-def-sfb-dc-setting], and let $\\Lambda$ be admissible in the sense of [sk-def-localizing-closure]. For $\\Lambda=\\overline{\\mathbf Q}_\\ell$, use the finite-subextension condensed ring of [sk-def-sfb-qlbar-condensed], as fixed by the chapter. Let $p:\\overline X\\to Y$ be proper, let\n$i:Y\\to\\overline Y$ be a quasi-compact open immersion, and let\n$\\overline X\\xrightarrow{j'}W\\xrightarrow{r}\\overline Y$ be a compactification of\n$i\\circ p$ in the sense of [sk-def-nagata-sketch-data], with $\\overline X$ dense in $W$. Then:\n\n1. $r^{-1}(Y)=\\overline X$ (as open subschemes of $W$), and $r$ restricts to $p$\nover $Y$;\n2. for $A\\in D(\\overline X_{\\mathrm{proét}},\\Lambda)$ there is a unique map\n$\\iota_A:i_!Rp_*A\\to Rr_*j'_!A$ in $D(\\overline Y_{\\mathrm{proét}},\\Lambda)$ whose\nrestriction to $Y$ is the identity of $Rp_*A$;\n3. $\\iota_A$ is an isomorphism for every $A\\in\\Loc(\\Dctf(\\overline X,\\Lambda))$."
 },
 {
  "step": 7,
  "node": "lem-sf-semilinear-proper-open-mate-pasting",
  "quote": "Let $C_1\\leftarrow C_2\\leftarrow\\cdots$ be any inverse sequence of commutative rings, and put $B_{n,T}=(C_n)_T$ on a fixed small skeleton of the pro-etale site of every scheme $T$ [def-weil-pbc-proetale-site]. Let $\\mathscr S_T$ be the category of strict semilinear module systems: an object consists of $B_{n,T}$-module sheaves $M_n$ and $B_{n+1,T}$-linear maps $M_{n+1}\\to\\operatorname{Res}_{n+1,n}M_n$; morphisms commute with transitions. Use $D(\\mathscr S_T)$ and its simultaneous section models [lem-coef-semilinear-derived-sections]. For a scheme map $v$, let $P_v$ be exact termwise inverse image and $S_v$ total derived direct image, with $P_v\\dashv S_v$, unit $\\eta_v$ and counit $\\varepsilon_v$. For an open immersion $a$, let $J_a\\dashv P_a$ be exact termwise extension by zero, with open unit the identity on sections over the open [lem-sf-open-tensor-normalization]. Direct-image compositors are mates of inverse-image compositors [lem-weil-pbc-mate-pasting].\n\nLet $Q$ be a commutative square of schemes with top arrow $k:U\\hookrightarrow V$, bottom arrow $i:W\\hookrightarrow Z$ quasi-compact open, vertical arrows $r:U\\to W$, $p:V\\to Z$ proper, and $pk=ir$ [def-weil-scheme-morphism-properties]. Set $V_W=V\\times_ZW$ with projections $b:V_W\\to V$, $q:V_W\\to W$, and $s=(k,r):U\\to V_W$ [def-pc-fibre-product]. Then $s$ is clopen. Let $\\pi_s:M\\to J_sP_sM$ be projection to the component $s(U)$, identity there and zero on its complementary open. Put\n$$\\alpha_Q:P_pJ_i\\xrightarrow{\\beta_{i,p}}J_bP_q\\xrightarrow{J_b\\pi_sP_q}J_bJ_sP_sP_q\\cong J_kP_r,$$\nwhere $\\beta$ is the open-unit-normalized map of [lem-sf-open-tensor-normalization], and define its proper mate\n$$\\Gamma_Q=(S_pJ_k\\varepsilon_r)\\circ(S_p\\alpha_QS_r)\\circ(\\eta_pJ_iS_r):J_iS_r\\to S_pJ_k.$$\nThere is a canonical isomorphism\n$$\\lambda_Q:P_iS_pJ_k\\cong S_qP_bJ_k\\cong S_qJ_s\\cong S_qS_s\\cong S_r.$$\nThe first isomorphism is right-adjoint conjugation of the cartesian open comparison $P_pJ_i\\cong J_bP_q$; the middle identification $J_s=S_s$ is the clopen section formula. The map $\\Gamma_Q$ is the $J_i\\dashv P_i$ adjunct of $\\lambda_Q^{-1}$, and $\\lambda_Q\\circ P_i\\Gamma_Q=1$ under the open unit.\n\nFor vertical pasting $Q_1$ above $Q_2$ with vertical maps $r_1,r_2$ on the left and $p_1,p_2$ on the right, and for horizontal pasting $Q_1$ left of $Q_2$ with top opens $k,l$, bottom opens $i,j$ and verticals $r,p,q$, respectively,\n$$\\Gamma_{Q_2Q_1}=(S_{p_2}\\Gamma_{Q_1})\\circ(\\Gamma_{Q_2}S_{r_1}),\\qquad\n\\Gamma_{Q_2Q_1}=(\\Gamma_{Q_2}J_k)\\circ(J_j\\Gamma_{Q_1}).$$\nFor base change $h:Z_1\\to Z$, write $a:V_1\\to V$, $c:W_1\\to W$, $d:U_1\\to U$ for the projections, and $Q_1$ for the induced square. Let $E_p:P_hS_p\\to S_{p_1}P_a$ and $E_r:P_cS_r\\to S_{r_1}P_d$ be the canonical pullback-square mates. Then\n$$(S_{p_1}\\beta_{k,a})\\circ(E_pJ_k)\\circ(P_h\\Gamma_Q)\n=(\\Gamma_{Q_1}P_d)\\circ(J_{i_1}E_r)\\circ(\\beta_{i,h}S_r).$$\nAll equations use the canonical compositors, hold in the semilinear derived categories before evaluation, and are independent of parenthesization. Identity verticals or identity horizontals give identity transformations. They apply also to noncartesian refinement squares, where $r=1$ and $s$ is the clopen section.\n\nIf a commutative ring $B$ has compatible maps $B\\to C_n$, termwise restriction to constant-$B$ module towers identifies $\\Gamma_Q$ with the normalized constant-tower mate of [lem-cbc-strict-tower-proper-open-calculus], via the canonical scalar-restriction direct-image and pullback comparisons of [lem-weil-pbc-ring-sheaf-change-adjunction]. Evaluation gives the same mate over $C_n$. When $C_n$ is finite and the evaluated input has a bounded-below etale-origin witness [def-weil-pbc-etale-origin-witness], it is the specified finite proper/open comparison. No faithfulness of derived evaluation or scalar restriction on morphisms is asserted."
 },
 {
  "step": 7,
  "node": "lem-sf-open-tensor-normalization",
  "quote": "Let $j:U\\hookrightarrow X$ be an open immersion of schemes, let\n$g:X'\\to X$, and form $U'=U\\times_X X'$ with $j':U'\\hookrightarrow X'$ and\n$g_U:U'\\to U$ [def-cbc-open-immersion-square]. On the small pro-etale sites\n[def-weil-pbc-proetale-site], let $B_X,B_{X'}$ be sheaves of commutative rings\nand $g^{-1}B_X\\to B_{X'}$ a ring map; put $B_U=j^{-1}B_X$ and\n$B_{U'}=j'^{-1}B_{X'}$, with the restricted structural ring map for $g_U$.\nWork in the ordinary unbounded derived categories of these module sheaves\n[def-weil-sheaf-of-modules-category, def-weil-abstract-derived-category].\nUse exact extension by zero $j_!,j'_!$ on the localized ringed sites\n[def-pur-localized-ringed-site, lem-pur-site-restriction-module-adjunction-exact],\ncanonical derived module pullback and tensor\n[lem-weil-pbc-ringed-topoi-derived-tensor-pullback], and the canonical\npullback-composition isomorphism $\\theta:j'^*Lg^*\\cong Lg_U^*j^*$.\n\nThe open adjunction units $u_M:M\\to j^*j_!M$ and $u'_{M'}:M'\\to j'^*j'_!M'$\nare isomorphisms, in the normalization whose section over an object factoring\nthrough the open is that same section. Extension by zero is fully faithful\non these derived categories. There are canonical natural isomorphisms\n$$\\beta_M:Lg^*j_!M\\xrightarrow{\\sim}j'_!Lg_U^*M,\\qquad\n\\gamma_{M,N}:j_!M\\otimes^L_{B_X}N\\xrightarrow{\\sim}\nj_!(M\\otimes^L_{B_U}j^*N).$$\nHere $M\\in D(B_U)$ and $N\\in D(B_X)$ are arbitrary. The inverse of $\\beta_M$\nis the $j'_!\\dashv j'^*$-adjunct of $Lg_U^*u_M$, followed by $\\theta^{-1}$;\nthe inverse of $\\gamma_{M,N}$ is the $j_!\\dashv j^*$-adjunct of\n$u_M\\otimes1$ under the canonical restriction/tensor comparison. After the\nopen unit identifications, $j'^*\\beta_M$ and $j^*\\gamma_{M,N}$ are the\nidentity maps under these specified pullback and tensor identifications.\nFor constant finite $B_X=B_{X'}=A$, with the canonical constant-section structural ring map, these are precisely the canonical maps of\n[lem-cbc-proetale-extension-zero-base-change] and\n[lem-cbc-extension-zero-projection-formula]. These assertions also hold for the ringed diagram topoi of arbitrary strict\ninverse coefficient systems. More explicitly, use the site\n$\\mathbf N\\times T_{\\mathrm{proet}}$ with topology generated by vertical\npro-etale covering families [lem-weil-pbc-diagram-site-realization]. A sheaf\nof commutative rings on this site is an inverse system $B_{0,T}\\leftarrow\nB_{1,T}\\leftarrow\\cdots$; its module sheaves are the strict semilinear\nmodule systems. Use their ordinary unbounded derived category, termwise\nopen extension, and total left derived system pullback and tensor.\nThe coefficient transitions and the structural ring maps are arbitrary;\nno flatness or completion is imposed."
 },
 {
  "step": 7,
  "node": "lem-weil-pbc-mate-pasting",
  "quote": "Notation of [def-weil-pbc-adjunction-mate]: for adjunctions $L_1\\dashv R_1$ ($L_1:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal B$), $L_2\\dashv R_2$ ($L_2:\\mathcal C\\to\\mathcal D$) and functors $G:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal C$, $H:\\mathcal B\\to\\mathcal D$, a *square* is a natural transformation $\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_1$ and $\\alpha^\\flat:GR_1\\to R_2H$ is its mate; conjugates $\\tau^c$ are as in [def-weil-pbc-adjunction-conjugate].\n\n1. (Pasting along $G,H$.) Let moreover $L_3\\dashv R_3$ ($L_3:\\mathcal E\\to\\mathcal F$), $G_2:\\mathcal C\\to\\mathcal E$, $H_2:\\mathcal D\\to\\mathcal F$, $\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_1$ and $\\beta:L_3G_2\\to H_2L_2$. The pasted square $\\gamma=(H_2\\alpha)\\circ(\\beta G):L_3G_2G\\to H_2HL_1$ has mate $\\gamma^\\flat=(\\beta^\\flat H)\\circ(G_2\\alpha^\\flat):G_2GR_1\\to G_2R_2H\\to R_3H_2H$.\n2. (Pasting along the adjunctions.) Let $L_1\\dashv R_1$ ($\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal B$), $L_3\\dashv R_3$ ($\\mathcal B\\to\\mathcal C$), $L_2\\dashv R_2$ ($\\mathcal D\\to\\mathcal E$), $L_4\\dashv R_4$ ($\\mathcal E\\to\\mathcal F$), $G:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal D$, $H:\\mathcal B\\to\\mathcal E$, $K:\\mathcal C\\to\\mathcal F$, $\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_1$, $\\beta:L_4H\\to KL_3$. With respect to the composite adjunctions $L_3L_1\\dashv R_1R_3$ and $L_4L_2\\dashv R_2R_4$ (unit $R_1\\eta_3L_1\\circ\\eta_1$, counit $\\varepsilon_3\\circ L_3\\varepsilon_1R_3$), the pasted square $\\delta=(\\beta L_1)\\circ(L_4\\alpha):L_4L_2G\\to KL_3L_1$ has mate $\\delta^\\flat=(R_2\\beta^\\flat)\\circ(\\alpha^\\flat R_3):GR_1R_3\\to R_2HR_3\\to R_2R_4K$.\n3. (Changing the adjunctions by a transformation.) (a) For a square $\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_1$ and a third adjunction $L_3\\dashv R_3$ with $L_3:\\mathcal C\\to\\mathcal D$ and $\\tau:L_3\\to L_2$, the square $\\alpha\\circ(\\tau G):L_3G\\to HL_1$ has mate (for $L_1\\dashv R_1$, $L_3\\dashv R_3$) equal to $(\\tau^cH)\\circ\\alpha^\\flat:GR_1\\to R_2H\\to R_3H$, $\\tau^c:R_2\\to R_3$. (b) For an adjunction $L_0\\dashv R_0$ with $L_0:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal B$, $\\sigma:L_1\\to L_0$, and a square $\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_1$, the square $(H\\sigma)\\circ\\alpha:L_2G\\to HL_0$ has mate (for $L_0\\dashv R_0$, $L_2\\dashv R_2$) equal to $\\alpha^\\flat\\circ(G\\sigma^c):GR_0\\to GR_1\\to R_2H$, $\\sigma^c:R_0\\to R_1$.\n4. (Identity.) For one adjunction $L\\dashv R$ and $G,H$ identity functors, the mate of $\\mathrm{id}_L$ is $\\mathrm{id}_R$."
 },
 {
  "step": 7,
  "node": "lem-pur-compact-support-condensed-realization",
  "quote": "Let $J_T:\\mathcal I_T(R)[1/t]\\simeq\\mathcal C_E^c(T)$ be the derived coefficient-extension equivalence of [lem-weil-pbc-condensed-e-realization]. For the coefficient ring $E_{\\overline X}^c$, let $j_{!,E}$ be sheafification of the extension-by-zero module presheaf on the open slice site, applied termwise to complexes [lem-pur-site-restriction-module-adjunction-exact]. Let $Rg_{*,E}$ be the derived direct image with the coefficient action and pullback identification of [def-weil-pbc-condensed-e-module-category]. Put $P_E^c=Rg_{*,E}j_{!,E}$ on those condensed module-object categories, and use the identical composite to define $P_\\Omega^c$ for the finite-extension-valued coefficient ring $\\Omega_T^c$ of [prop-weil-pbc-qbar-condensed-two-colimit], $\\Omega=\\overline{\\mathbf Q}_\\ell$. These composites preserve the indicated bounded constructible categories. There is a natural isomorphism\n$$a_{f,E,K}:J_Y((P_RK)[1/t])\\xrightarrow{\\sim}P_E^cJ_X(K[1/t]).$$\nFor finite $E\\subset F\\subset\\Omega$ and also for $F=\\Omega$, put $T_{E,F}^T=F_T^c\\otimes^L_{E_T^c}-$ (with $F_T^c=\\Omega_T^c$ in the latter case). The canonical coefficient projection comparison is an isomorphism\n$$T_{E,F}^YP_E^c\\xrightarrow{\\sim}P_F^cT_{E,F}^X.$$\nFor finite $F/E$, write $K_S$ for completed integral scalar extension to $S=\\mathcal O_F$ [def-weil-integral-scalar-extension]. There is a canonical comparison $(P_RK)_S\\simeq P_SK_S$, compatible with $\\alpha$, $a$, and the preceding condensed projection comparison after rationalization. All these specified comparisons preserve morphisms, shifts, identities, towers of finite coefficient extensions and compactification-refinement maps, with their composition coherences. This is a comparison of compact-support functors only; it makes no assertion about their right adjoints."
 },
 {
  "step": 7,
  "node": "lem-zl-compact-support-realization-factor-normalization",
  "quote": "The specified comparison $a_{f,E,K}$ of [lem-pur-compact-support-condensed-realization] is exactly\n$$a_{f,E,K}=Rg_{*,E}(\\delta^J_{j,K})\\circ\\chi^J_{g,j_{!,B}K}\\circ J_Y(q_{c,K}^{-1}[1/t]).$$\nHere $\\chi^J_g:J_YRg_{*,B}\\xrightarrow{\\sim}Rg_{*,E}J_Z$ is the direct-image comparison of $J$, after rationalization; $\\delta^J_j:J_Zj_{!,B}\\xrightarrow{\\sim}j_{!,E}J_X$ is the scalar/open comparison whose restriction to the open is the identity under the balanced tensor identifications, equivalently whose inverse is the open-adjunction adjunct of the open unit [lem-sf-open-tensor-normalization].\n\nFor the same specified compactification, the coefficient projection comparison $\\xi_{f,E,F}:T_YP_E^c\\xrightarrow{\\sim}P_F^cT_X$ of [lem-pur-compact-support-condensed-realization] is exactly\n$$\\xi_{f,E,F,K}=Rg_{*,F}(\\delta^{E,F}_{j,K})\\circ\\pi_{g,j_{!,E}K}.$$\nThe open map $\\delta^{E,F}_j:T_Zj_{!,E}\\xrightarrow{\\sim}j_{!,F}T_X$ is again identity on the open under scalar balancing. The proper map $\\pi_{g,L}:T_YRg_{*,E}L\\to Rg_{*,F}T_ZL$ is the $Lg_F^*\\dashv Rg_{*,F}$-adjunct of the specific composite\n$$Lg_F^*T_YRg_{*,E}L\\xrightarrow{\\mu}T_ZLg_E^*Rg_{*,E}L\\xrightarrow{T_Z\\varepsilon_{g,E,L}}T_ZL,$$\nwhere $\\mu$ is canonical pullback/tensor balancing. This formula also holds for $F=\\Omega$, as an equality in the $F^c$-module derived category, and $\\pi_g$ is invertible on the stated domain. For an identity open $j$, both displayed compact-support comparisons reduce to their specified proper factors, with the identity open factor. The displayed maps are natural in $K$; no endomorphism, equality of proper-unit compact-support operators, or point realization is asserted."
 },

 {
  "step": 8,
  "node": "sk-def-sfb-dc-lower-shriek",
  "quote": "Fix a prime $\\ell$ and a field $k$ of characteristic $p>0$ with $p\\ne\\ell$. All schemes below are separated and of finite type over $k$, and all morphisms are $k$-morphisms, as in [sk-def-sfb-dc-setting]. Write $D_{\\mathrm{cons}}(T,\\Lambda)\\subseteq D(\\Mod_{\\Lambda_T})$ for the bounded constructible derived category of module objects over the pulled-back coefficient ring $\\Lambda_T$, with its inclusion functor, as defined there. Let $\\Lambda$ be a commutative condensed ring\nfor which the derived pushforwards restrict to bounded constructible complexes\n([sk-def-sfb-dc-restricted-functors]; for instance $\\Lambda=\\underline{\\cO}_E,\\underline E,\\underline{\\Qlb}$).\nFor a morphism $f\\colon X\\to Y$ and a compactification $c=(X\\xrightarrow{i}Z\\xrightarrow{p}Y)$\nof $f$, the *lower shriek attached to $c$* is the composite of the two restricted functors of\n[sk-def-sfb-dc-restricted-functors]:\n\n$$\nRf_!^c:=Rp_*\\circ i_!\\colon D_{\\mathrm{cons}}(X,\\Lambda)\\to D_{\\mathrm{cons}}(Y,\\Lambda).\n$$\n\nThe *derived lower shriek* is $Rf_!:=Rf_!^{c_f}$ for the chosen compactification $c_f$ of\n[sk-def-sfb-dc-setting]. No separate preservation statement is needed for $Rf_!$.\n\nThe functor $Rf_!^c$ is compatible with the ambient lower shriek\n$Rf_!^c=R(p_*)\\circ D(i_!)$ on $D(\\Mod_{\\Lambda_X})$ of [sk-def-sfb-module-derived-lower-shriek]:\nthere is a natural isomorphism $\\iota\\circ Rf_!^c\\cong Rf_!^c\\circ\\iota$, with $\\iota$ the inclusions\nof $D_{\\mathrm{cons}}$. In particular $Rf_!$ on $D_{\\mathrm{cons}}$ is compatible with the ambient\n$Rf_!$ for the chosen compactification."
 },
 {
  "step": 8,

 },
 {
  "step": 9,
  "node": "lem-weil-pbc-geometric-field-proetale-evaluation",
  "quote": "Use [def-weil-size-convention]. Let $K$ be an algebraically closed field [def-weil-algebraic-closure], and let $Z=\\operatorname{Spec}K$ [def-pc-scheme]. The functor $S\\mapsto Z[S]$ of [lem-weil-pbc-profinite-test-object-functor] identifies sheaves on $Z_{\\mathrm{proet}}$ [def-weil-pbc-proetale-site] with sheaves on profinite sets with finite jointly surjective coverings. Under this identification, the inverse image $p_Z^*$ of [lem-weil-pbc-profinite-point-pullback] is the identity on sheaves on profinite sets, and evaluation at $Z$ is evaluation at the one-point profinite set. Every pro-etale covering of $Z$ admits a section through one member. Evaluation at $Z$ is exact on abelian sheaves, and on sheaves of modules [def-weil-sheaf-of-modules-category] over any ring sheaf $R$ it preserves finite free modules and cokernels, with values in modules over $R(Z)$. For every extension $K\\subseteq K'$ of algebraically closed fields, scheme inverse image [def-weil-proetale-inverse-image] corresponds to the identity under these profinite-site identifications, and therefore canonically preserves evaluation at the final object. These identifications are compatible with composition and with ring and module structures."
 },
 {
  "step": 9,
  "node": "lem-zl-condensed-point-supertrace",
  "quote": "Let $\\ell$ be prime, let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of positive prime characteristic $p\\ne\\ell$, put $s=\\operatorname{Spec}k$, and let $E/\\mathbf Q_\\ell$ be a finite extension inside $\\Omega=\\overline{\\mathbf Q}_\\ell$ [def-weil-l-adic-numbers, def-weil-l-adic-coefficient-extension, def-weil-affine-spectrum]. Let $\\underline\\Omega=\\operatorname{Profinite.padicAlgClRingObj}(\\ell)$ be the finite-subextension-valued condensed ring of [sk-def-sfb-qlbar-condensed], and put $U_s=p_s^*\\underline\\Omega$, the tensor unit of $\\mathcal D_s=D^b_c(s)[[\\underline\\Omega]]$ [sk-def-sfb-pulled-back-coefficients, sk-def-sfb-bounded-constructible, def-sfb-dc-tensor]. Use the scalar inclusion $E\\to\\Omega$ and its pulled-back coefficient map [def-sfb-subfield-to-qlbar-coefficient-map]. On a bounded finite-dimensional $E$-complex, define $\\rho_{E,s}$ by replacing each term $E^r$, after choosing a basis, by $U_s^{\\oplus r}$, and replacing each differential or chain map matrix by the same matrix acting through $E\\to\\Omega$. Use total tensor products and Koszul symmetry. This construction is independent of bases up to its canonical change-of-basis isomorphisms, sends quasi-isomorphisms to isomorphisms, and defines an exact strong symmetric monoidal functor\n$$\\rho_{E,s}:D^b_{\\mathrm{fd}}(E)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal D_s,$$\nwhere $D^b_{\\mathrm{fd}}(E)$ has the meaning of [def-weil-point-fd-complexes]. It identifies $\\rho_{E,s}(E[0])$ with $U_s$ and satisfies $H^i\\rho_{E,s}(C)\\cong\\rho_{E,s}(H^i(C)[0])$. The latter cohomology module evaluated at the geometric point has underlying vector space $H^i(C)\\otimes_E\\Omega$."
